**Claim.** An OSError on the copy (Windows MAX_PATH under an MO2 mod tree is the realistic one) turns an authored-physics piece into a physics-free piece with 'converted' status. This is the BUG-00 lineage (physics lookup fails OPEN) one step later in the same function, and the 66-of-121 'physics present but dead' survey would be the only place it shows — after the fact.

**Evidence.** L24697-24715: `try: ... if not _done: atomic_copy(str(src_xml), str(dst_xml_disk))` / L24714-24715 `except Exception:\n    pass`, immediately followed by L24716-24717 `if not dst_xml_disk.is_file(): return False  # nothing to point at (no source + no generated)`. The outer handler L24886-24899 (voiced since 08-22) never sees it because nothing raised past L24715. The caller L8620-8629 discards the bool, and the outer handler's own comment L24889-24891 admits 'the caller swallows the return'.

**Verifier.** CONFIRMED mechanics: nif_convert.py L24697 `try:` ... L24713 `atomic_copy(str(src_xml), str(dst_xml_disk))` / L24714-24715 `except Exception:\n pass`, then L24716-24717 `if not dst_xml_disk.is_file(): return False`. The single caller L8624 `_finalize_hdt_physics(dst_path, src_path)` discards the bool (its handler L8625-8629 only records a RAISED exception). Nothing downstream fires: the later passes resolve the dst pointer via `_read_source_hdt_xml_text_uncached`, whose `hdt_xml_unresolved` record (L25277-25281) is preceded by the `#xml-source-of-truth` recovery `if _PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT: return _PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` (L25270-25271), so the source binding HIDES the missing dst copy. The structural pin cannot see it either: PASS_HINTS has no `atomic_copy`/`atomic_write_bytes`. CORRECTIONS: (1) 'physics-free piece' is overstated. Both phases attach the pointer BEFORE finalize (phase-1 L7449 `hdt_xml = _find_hdt_xml_for_armor(src_path)` -> L7524-7529 `NiStringExtraData.New(... string_value=hdt_xml)`; phase-2 L30121-30127), and finalize only ADDS a pointer when none exists (L24732-24738 `if not has:`). A failed copy therefore ships the piece pointing at the SOURCE-relative authored path (VFS-resolvable while the source mod is active) or at the generated `<stem>.xml` left intact by the atomic copy -- but with the collider re-import, `_harden_hdt_xml_for_fsmp` and `_make_chains_static` all skipped: the outer handler's own words L24888-24891 'drops the authored-XML attach, the collider re-import AND the FSMP hardening in one go (dead skirts / skirt collapse / equip-CTD gate skipped)'. That is the real consequence. (2) The realistic vector is not MAX_PATH (unverified) but the MEASURED swap race: atomic_io.py L50-57 '24 `.tri` writes lost this race ... SEVERAL NIFs regenerate the SAME file (one armour's `_0`/`_1` plus its 1st-person variants)' -- `<stem>.xml` is shared the same way (L24668-24675 strips `_0`/`_1`), and `_swap_into_place` L64-83 raises `OutputLockedError(OSError)` after 6 retries, which this handler swallows. (3) 'voiced since 08-22' is wrong for the outer handler: `git log -S"physics finalize FAILED"` -> b94e0a8 'audit 2026-07-28'; the 08-22 census voiced the collider-loop try (IN_GAME_BUGS.md L3390 `_finalize_hdt_physics :22957 guards _copy_shape + warp`). (4) The second improvement (record at the L24717 `return False` when `src_xml is not None`) is redundant once the handler records: with a source present, the only way to reach that return without the handler firing is impossible (copy/sanitise either wrote the file or raised).

**Fix.** `except Exception as _e: _note_pass_failure("_finalize_hdt_physics/xml-copy", _e, dst_path)` (label style matches the existing `/collider:{cn}` sub-labels). Drop the second part (recording at the L24717 return) -- redundant once the handler records. Optionally add `atomic_copy`/`atomic_write_bytes` to PASS_HINTS in tests/test_no_silent_pass_failures.py so IO handlers around physics files are pinned too (check the >=40-helper sanity test still passes).

**Proof it landed.** Handler-only; converted vertex/weight/bone/XML bytes unchanged (the recorder only appends to `_PASS_FAILURES_THIS_PIECE` -> `ConvertResult.reason`). Vertex parity via single-harness `exe_parity_convert.py` A/B (rule 5). Raise-injection: monkeypatch `nc.atomic_copy` to raise `OutputLockedError`, run `_finalize_hdt_physics` on a fixture whose source declares an authored XML and whose dst has NO `<stem>.xml`, assert the `/xml-copy` entry is in `nc._piece_pass_failures()` and the function returned False; a second case with a pre-existing generated `<stem>.xml` must ALSO record (the file survives, but the authored chains were not installed).

**Prior art.** new -- BUG-00 (project_hdt_xml_fail_open.md) fixed the LOOKUP; b94e0a8 (07-28) voiced the outermost handler; the 08-22 census (IN_GAME_BUGS.md L3385-3430) voiced the collider-loop try; this copy handler was never in scope because `atomic_copy` is not a PASS_HINT.

### F024 [HIGH] Copy path's 258-line fit try discards warp/groove/snap/chain-blend into `snapped = None` with no record; phase 2 records each; the ratchet test exempts assignment-only handlers by definition
`src/nif_convert.py:7183` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens two-path-drift · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** On 74% of the pack a raised warp/groove/chain-blend ships the shape as SOURCE geometry with an empty `reason`, indistinguishable from 'nothing to fit'. The project's own rule (memory: 'a swallowed exception is indistinguishable from nothing qualified'; failures must ride `reason` via `_note_pass_failure` because the worker's stderr is lost) is honoured on one path and violated on the other, and the in-code comment describes a guard that does not exist.

**Evidence.** L7183-7184: `except Exception:\n    snapped = None` closes the try opened at L6925 that wraps warp (L6933), `_slot_aware_inflation_magnitude` (L6946), `_smooth_warp_grooves` (L7084, NO inner try despite the comment at L7079-7083 saying it is 'Guarded like _gc_bn'), `snap_armor_outside_body` (L7093) and `_physics_chain_nowarp_blend` (L7181). Phase 2 records every one: L28905-28906 `failed.append((f"{s.name}:warp", repr(e)))`, L28916-28917 `failed.append((f"{s.name}:snap", ...))`, L28903-28904 `_note_pass_failure("_smooth_warp_grooves", _pe)`. Same pattern at the reskin: copy L7234-7235 `except Exception:\n    override_skin_p1 = None` vs phase 2 L29627-29628 `failed.append((f"{s.name}:reskin-compute", ...))`; z-fight: copy L7293-7294 `except Exception:\n    pass  # z-fight fix is best-effort` vs phase 2 L29661-29662 `failed.append(("zfight-fix", repr(e)))`. AST probe: 19 silent handlers in convert_nif vs 18 in phase2, but phase 2's are all leaf lookups; the copy path's L7183 covers the entire chain.

**Verifier.** CONFIRMED in substance. src/nif_convert.py L6925 opens a 258-line try whose only handler is L7183-7184 `except Exception:\n    snapped = None`. AST probe of that Try: nested tries cover [6949-6983, 6997-7018, 7037-7059, 7075-7076, 7125-7135, 7142-7172] (inflate, conform, _gc_bn, the two #panel-rigidity blocks); calls NOT under any nested try = warp_armor_by_body_delta L6933, _slot_aware_inflation_magnitude L6946, _smooth_warp_grooves L7084, snap_armor_outside_body L7093, _physics_chain_nowarp_blend L7181 (plus every _stage_p1 checkpoint). On raise, L7240-7243 ships `sv_world` with `verts_modified=False` and NOTHING is appended to `failed` or `_PASS_FAILURES_THIS_PIECE`, so `reason` (L7798 join) is empty and status is 'converted (copy)'. Phase 2's mirror try L28773-28904 is voiced: handler L28905 `failed.append((f"{s.name}:warp", ...))`, snap L28916, groove L28903 `_note_pass_failure`, reskin L29627, z-fight L29661 -- versus copy L7234-7235 `override_skin_p1 = None` and L7293-7294 `pass  # z-fight fix is best-effort`. Confirmed the outer handler is visible to nothing: the project's ratchet tests/test_no_silent_pass_failures.py `_is_silent` (L59-73) defines silent as 'only pass/continue/break/return-literal' and its docstring says 'Any call, assignment or raise records something and is not silent' -- so `snapped = None` is exempt BY DEFINITION even though its guarded calls (`warp_`, `_smooth`) match PASS_HINTS L44-54. Every prior census ('7 truly-silent handlers' IN_GAME_BUGS.md L3384-3442, 'Silent swallows around mesh passes: ZERO' project_current_state.md L1625, settled 'outer mutes inner class at ZERO') used that same definition, so this handler was never counted -- yet it is exactly the 'voiced inner inside a mute outer' shape feedback_method_traps.md L24-32 warns about: the L7173-7174 `_note_pass_failure("panel-rigidity/phase1")` sits INSIDE the mute L6925 try. The code knows the hazard: commit e8a9133 added 'the big phase-1 fit try whose handler sets `snapped = None`, so an escape here would discard the warp, inflate AND conform' (L7153-7156 today) and gave the NEW block its own try instead of voicing the outer one; IN_GAME_BUGS.md L3476 repeats it. No memory/worklog/CHANGELOG item lists the outer handler as open or accepted -> new. CORRECTIONS: (1) the L7079-7083 comment is not a description of a guard for the groove call that never existed -- git show 20c84ca shows it was written above `try: _gc_nip = _body_nipple_weight(...)`, which was later deleted (`_gc_nip` has 0 hits in src/), leaving the comment orphaned over the unguarded `_smooth_warp_grooves` call; same conclusion (stale comment, unguarded call), different cause. (2) The '19 vs 18' count is not reproducible: my probe (broad handler whose body is only pass/continue/break/return-literal/`x = None`, nested defs excluded) gives 17 in convert_nif vs 17 in convert_nif_phase2; the number is not load-bearing -- the difference is what each handler covers (copy L7183 = 258 lines / the whole chain; phase 2's largest is 44 lines) and that is confirmed. (3) '74%' = 73.9% copy path (433/1658 body-swap, project_fitchain_coverage.md) -- correct as the population the handler applies to, not a measured failure rate; how often the chain actually raises is unmeasured. Rules: sound as a fix (fix is reporting only, S, vert-neutral -- provided the added `failed.append`/`_note_pass_failure` sits inside the handler, per the 'every added line inside an ast.ExceptHandler' proof used at BF9C59ED); reframe the proof to ALSO extend `_is_silent` (or add a sibling check) so `x = None`-style discards of the try's result are counted, otherwise the ratchet stays blind to the class and the next one reappears.

**Fix.** In the L7183 handler: `except Exception as e: failed.append((f"{s.name}:fit-chain", repr(e))); snapped = None` (or `_note_pass_failure("phase1-fit-chain", e, dst_path)`); give L7084 `_smooth_warp_grooves` and L7181 `_physics_chain_nowarp_blend` their own try/`_note_pass_failure` as phase 2 does (L28895-28904); voice L7234-7235 (`reskin-compute`) and L7293-7294 (`zfight-fix`) with the phase-2 strings; delete or rewrite the orphaned L7079-7083 comment (its subject `_gc_nip` no longer exists). Then extend tests/test_no_silent_pass_failures.py `_is_silent` so a handler whose body is only `<name> = None` over a try that calls a PASS_HINTS helper is flagged -- the current definition is what let this one through.

**Proof it landed.** Reporting-only, but rule 5: `scripts/golden_output.py capture` then `check` on the current tree plus a copy-path sample under PYTHONHASHSEED=1, --workers=1, absolute mod dir; per-shape vertex max|delta| 0.0, only `reason` differs. Negative control on a scratch COPY: monkeypatch `_smooth_warp_grooves` to raise and assert the piece's `reason` names it and the shape ships `verts_modified=False` (today it ships silently). Also assert the extended `_is_silent` reports L7183 when the recorder line is removed (guard-of-the-guard, as test_the_check_can_actually_fail does).

**Prior art.** Handler acknowledged as a hazard in code (L7153-7156, added e8a9133 2026-08-23; IN_GAME_BUGS.md L3476) and worked AROUND by giving new inner blocks their own try; never listed as open, never counted by the 08-22 census or tests/test_no_silent_pass_failures.py because both define silent as pass/continue/return only.

### F025 [HIGH] Copy-path `_copy_shape` retry silently ships source verts + source skin as 'converted (copy)'; the fallback may also duplicate a half-built shape; phase 2 drops instead
`src/nif_convert.py:7417` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens two-path-drift · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** A copy-path shape whose fitted write fails ships with the CBBE-source verts AND the CBBE-source skin (no reskin, no scale bones) while the piece reports 'converted (copy)' with a clean reason. That is exactly the 'shipped UNFITTED verbatim copy' condition the same function records for the whole piece at L6551-6552, missed at the per-shape level. It is silently wrong output in a real run.

**Evidence.** L7413-7425: `try: _copy_shape(s, dst_nif_for_fit, override_verts=override_v, override_skin=j["override_skin"])\nexcept Exception:\n    try:\n        _copy_shape(s, dst_nif_for_fit)\n    except Exception as _e2:\n        failed.append((s.name, f"DROPPED (copy failed): {_e2!r}"))`. Nothing is appended when the SECOND call succeeds. Phase 2 L30006-30010: `except Exception as e: failed.append((s.name, f"DROPPED (copy failed): {e!r}")); continue`. No test references either string (grep of tests/ for 'DROPPED (copy failed)' and '_copy_shape(s, dst_nif_for_fit)' returned nothing).

**Verifier.** CONFIRMED. src/nif_convert.py L7413-7425: `try: _copy_shape(s, dst_nif_for_fit, override_verts=override_v, override_skin=j["override_skin"])` / `except Exception: try: _copy_shape(s, dst_nif_for_fit) except Exception as _e2: failed.append((s.name, f"DROPPED (copy failed): {_e2!r}"))`. When the second call succeeds nothing is appended, the first exception is discarded unbound, and `_copy_shape` with override_verts=None / override_skin=None takes the source-verts and `elif src_shape.bone_names:` verbatim-source-skin branch (L23327-23342) -- so the shape ships CBBE-source geometry AND CBBE-source skin, without the compute_body_blend_skinning reskin or the `add_scale_bone_weights` graft the copy path computed at L7206-7227. `dropped_shapes` (L7800-7801) keys on `msg.startswith("DROPPED")`, so auto_convert's PARTIAL bucket cannot see it either; status stays 'converted (copy)' with a clean reason. Phase 2 L30006-30010 makes the opposite choice: `failed.append((s.name, f"DROPPED (copy failed): {e!r}")); continue` -- no fallback. The differential surface is real: the fitted call runs code the plain copy does not (override-verts seam weld / normal recompute from L23037, `_cap_skin_bone_count`, `_install_skin` L2349-2417 with pynifly `skin()/add_bone/set_skin_to_bone_xform/setShapeWeights`), so 'first fails, second succeeds' is a live class, not a theoretical one. ADDITIONAL hazard the finding under-states: `_copy_shape` has no cleanup on failure (no finally/remove in L22998-23448), and `createShapeFromData` runs at L23167 BEFORE the skin install at L23339 -- so a raise inside `_install_skin` leaves a half-built shape named s.name in dst_nif and the fallback then creates a SECOND shape with the same name (one fitted-with-broken-skin, one source). Could not execute to confirm pynifly accepts the duplicate name; flag as plausible, not proven. Prior art: commit a0c621d ('phase-1 AND phase-2 shape-copy loops no longer silently drop a shape') and memory project_converter_hardening.md Tier 2 only voiced the DOUBLE failure; neither names the single-failure fallback. The ratchet test cannot see L7417 because its handler body is a Try, not pass/continue/return (`_is_silent` L59-73 returns False for any other statement). rg for 'DROPPED (copy failed)' and '_copy_shape(s, dst_nif_for_fit)' over tests/ scripts/ docs/: 0 hits (only src/nif_convert.py, 5) -> no test pins either branch. Nothing in memory, IN_GAME_BUGS.md archive, CHANGELOG or worklog covers it -> new. Rules: the recording half is sound (S, vert-neutral, reporting only). REFRAME the second half: 'decide whether phase 2 adopts the fallback' is a BEHAVIOUR change on the body-swap path (a shape that today is absent would ship as source geometry) -- it needs its own in-game verdict and must not ride the reporting fix; and the fallback itself should first clear the duplicate-shape question above (either roll back the half-built shape before retrying, or refuse the retry when dst_nif already holds s.name).

**Fix.** Bind and record the first failure: `except Exception as _e1: ... try: _copy_shape(s, dst_nif_for_fit) ... failed.append((s.name, f"UNFITTED (fitted copy failed, shipped source verts+skin): {_e1!r}"))` on the success branch, and have the DROPPED branch include `_e1` too. Add an `unfitted_shapes` bucket to ConvertResult/auto_convert beside `dropped_shapes`. Before retrying, check `dst_nif` for an existing shape named `s.name` (left by a raise after L23167) and either remove it or route to DROPPED rather than creating a twin. Leave phase 2's drop-vs-fallback choice as a separate, verdict-gated behaviour change.

**Proof it landed.** Convert-based: golden `check` + copy-path sample under PYTHONHASHSEED=1, --workers=1, absolute mod dir; vertex/weight identical, only `reason` changes. Negative control on a scratch copy of the source: monkeypatch `_install_skin` to raise once for one shape; assert the reason carries UNFITTED, the output NIF holds exactly ONE shape of that name (this is the assertion that will expose the duplicate-shape hazard if pynifly allows it), and `dropped_shapes` is empty. Then remove the recorder line and assert the test fails.

**Prior art.** new -- a0c621d/project_converter_hardening.md voiced only the double-failure branch; single-failure fallback never named anywhere

### F004 [MEDIUM] GUI settings JSON is written non-atomically (truncate+write) and a malformed file silently loads as defaults, is displayed as defaults, and is overwritten as defaults on the next toggle -- the sibling exclusions JSON already got the atomic fix for this class
`src/gui_settings.py:1526` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens io-encoding-determinism+packaging-deploy · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** A crash/kill/full disk during any toggle-save leaves a truncated JSON; the exe then reads the GUI settings json -> env at import (rule 3) as PURE DEFAULTS and every promoted-or-not choice is gone, silently, for the whole run - the exact 'run looked completely normal for an hour, the work simply did not happen' failure the file's own docstring (1480-1486) and feedback_deployed_build_runs_at_defaults describe, now reachable through a torn file instead of an absent key. The project's own atomic convention is applied to the exclusions JSON but not here.

**Evidence.** 1526: `p.write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2, sort_keys=True), encoding="utf-8")` (no temp, no os.replace; grep for `.tmp|.bak|os.replace` in gui_settings.py: 0 hits). 1457 docstring: `malformed/absent file -> pure defaults`; 1461-1463: `raw = json.loads(p.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))` / `except Exception:` / `return vals`. 1496-1498 (`unseen_settings`): `except Exception:` / `return True, []` - the 'new options' warning is also silenced. src/gui.py:810-814 `def _settings_set(key, value): ... gui_settings.save_values(state["settings"])` saves on EVERY control change. Contrast src/exclusions.py:136 `atomic_write_bytes(` for the sibling JSON.

**Verifier.** CONFIRMED in code, with two scope corrections and a severity downgrade. `save_values` writes with `p.write_text(...)` (1526; rg for atomic|os.replace|.tmp|.bak in gui_settings.py = 0 hits) -- open('w') truncates first, so a kill/crash/disk-full mid-write leaves an empty or partial file. `load_values` (1461-1463) `except Exception: return vals` returns pure defaults for ANY parse failure with no signal, and `unseen_settings` (1496-1498) returns `(True, [])` for the same file, so both the preflight WARN (preflight.py:402-409) and the run-log NOTE (auto_convert.py:959-969) stay silent. The sibling exclusions JSON got exactly this fix for exactly this reason: exclusions.py:132-136 'Atomic (temp+rename): a torn write here would leave a truncated JSON that load() degrades to empty() -> silently dropping EVERY saved exclusion' (c74ebcd, 2026-07-05, one day after gui_settings landed in 8f6fbe3) -- so the project already classed this failure as real and fixed one of two files. Corrections: (1) the path is NOT 'the exe reads the json -> env at import': the headless `auto` command never reads the settings json (phase1_settled: only the GUI child launch applies it). The real path is gui.py:450 `state["settings"] = gui_settings.load_values()` at GUI start -> the Settings tab DISPLAYS defaults (so it is not fully invisible if the user looks) -> gui.py:2486 `apply_env(state.get("settings") ...)` runs the child at defaults -> and the very next control change (`_settings_set` 810-812, `_on_theme` 1395-1398) `save_values` overwrites the torn file with defaults + a full `_known_settings`, after which the loss is PERMANENT and `unseen_settings` reports nothing new. (2) Severity high -> medium: the trigger is a kill inside a few-KB write during a toggle (rare); the consequence is the documented expensive class, which justifies the S-effort fix. Also the finding's risk=behaviour is wrong under the honesty rule: with an intact settings file the env, and therefore every converted vertex/weight/bone/XML byte, is identical -- the only behavioural change is GUI/preflight messaging on a malformed file -> vert-neutral. Rules reframe: the improvement's 'raise' on an unparseable existing file must NOT raise from `load_values` at gui.py:450 (it would crash the GUI at startup); return defaults plus a parse-failure status (or a `load_status()`), surface it as a preflight FAIL/GUI banner offering the `.bak`, and make `unseen_settings` return an unknown state instead of `(True, [])`. Prior art: none -- rg over memory/, docs/worklog, CHANGELOG.md, IN_GAME_BUGS.md for settings x (atomic|torn|truncat|corrupt|malformed) = 0 hits; tests/test_gui_settings.py pins absent-file->defaults (line 134) but has no malformed-file case, so the fall-through is untested behaviour, not a pinned design decision.

**Fix.** Write through `atomic_io.atomic_write_bytes` (same-dir temp + os.replace) keeping a `.bak` of the previous good file. Do NOT raise from `load_values` (gui.py:450 calls it at GUI startup); return defaults plus a parse-failure status (or add `load_status()`), have `unseen_settings` return an unknown/failed state instead of `(True, [])`, and surface a parse failure as a preflight FAIL and GUI banner that offers the `.bak` -- so a settings loss is loud before the first toggle re-saves defaults over it.

**Proof it landed.** tests/test_gui_settings.py: (1) save, truncate the file mid-object, `load_values` returns defaults AND the status/`load_status` reports parse failure (today: silent -- the test fails, which is the point); (2) `unseen_settings` on that file does not return `(True, [])`; (3) a well-formed file round-trips text-identical through the atomic path and `.bak` holds the prior content; (4) a `_settings_set` after a torn load does not silently cement defaults (banner state set). No convert needed; with the same settings file the env is identical so converted output is unchanged by construction -- one paired convert (deployed exe, same json) may be run as the rule-5 vertex compare if the owner wants it on record.

**Prior art.** new -- no memory, worklog, CHANGELOG or bug-log entry; the class was recognised and fixed for src/exclusions.py only (c74ebcd, 2026-07-05).

### F007 [MEDIUM] PRECONDITION C: the four `global`-rebound singletons must be rebound on the `nc` namespace and `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT`'s writer/reader kept in ONE module -- a forked binding fails CLOSED (over-protects every declared-physics piece, loud `hdt_xml_unresolved`), not open
`src/nif_convert.py:25184` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens monolith-seams · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** A `global X; X = v` rebinds the dict of the module that OWNS the function. If the writer moves to a sub-module and the reader stays (or vice versa), or if the shim re-exports X by value, the reader sees the import-time value forever. For `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` that value is None, so `_hdt_collider_shape_names` (L25303) and `_hdt_softbody_shape_names` (L25083) fall through to the destination-pointer resolution that BUG-00 documented losing, return the EMPTY SET, and every `if s.name in collider_names: continue` guard disarms at once — the free-fall class, reproduced by a refactor and invisible to a byte compare of the source. Same shape as the memory's 'a cached lookup is decided by its first caller', in a second costume: decided by which MODULE the first caller lives in. The dict caches are safer (mutated in place) but any test that replaces them by assignment goes vacuous once the accessor moves.

**Evidence.** L25184 `global _PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` (writer, `_hdt_xml_bind_piece_source`); L25270-25271 `if _PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT:` / `return _PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` (the only reader, inside `_read_source_hdt_xml_text_uncached` L25241, with the comment 'losing that race used to fail OPEN (BUG-00)'); L24240 `global _SKELETON_BONES_CACHE`; L24280 `global _SKELETON_PARENTS_CACHE`; L10078 `global _SKELETON_BONES_NORM_CACHE` — in `_actor_can_resolve_bone` (L10044), 14,000 lines and three banner sections away from `_SKELETON_BONES_CACHE` (L24221) it keys on by identity. Tests assign these on the module: tests/test_smp_structural_relax.py L77 `nc._SKELETON_BONES_CACHE = set(SKELETON)` and L127; tests/test_hdt_xml_source_of_truth.py L116 `nc._PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT = XML` (plus L71,73,131,149,165). AST: 39 mutable containers, 4 `global` statements, `_BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE` replaced by assignment in tests (test_audit_2026_07_04.py L292/298/308 `monkeypatch.setattr(nc, "_BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE", {})`).

**Verifier.** The Python mechanics and the test-coupling are confirmed: exactly 4 `global` statements (ast.Global count 4: L10078, L24240, L24280, L25184), 4 None-initialised singletons (L24221, L24225, L24266, L25130), and tests that rebind them on the `nc` namespace (test_smp_structural_relax L77/78/127/128/194/195, test_hdt_xml_source_of_truth L71/73/116/131/149/165). A function's `global X; X = v` rebinds ITS OWN module dict, and a by-value re-export forks the name -- correct. BUT the headline consequence is REFUTED by the code at the cited line: losing `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` does NOT reproduce BUG-00's empty-set free-fall. In `_read_source_hdt_xml_text_uncached` the bound text is consulted only AFTER `_nif_declares_hdt_xml` returned True (L25266 `if not _nif_declares_hdt_xml(...): return None` precedes L25270 `if _PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT:`), and when it is None the function records a pass failure (L25279-25283 `_note_pass_failure("hdt_xml_unresolved", ... "FAIL CLOSED for this piece")`) and returns None, whereupon BOTH shape-name lookups take `if not txt: return _hdt_protect_all_or_none(...)` (L25096-25097, L25304-25306), which for a declaring NIF returns `{s.name for s in _nfq.shapes}` -- EVERY shape protected (L25117-25123, `#hdt-fail-closed`, same commit 3b714ed as the binding). So a forked singleton yields OVER-protection (jiggle/collider grafts skipped on every declared-physics piece on the destination-side lookups -- the memory's measured 'fail-closed only prevents it but over-protects, Top 27->22') plus a loud `hdt_xml_unresolved` note per piece, not the free-fall and not silent. The finding's own quoted comment says 'USED TO fail OPEN'. It is also not invisible to the suite: `test_destination_lookup_recovers_from_the_bound_source` (L107-120) asserts the collider set == {"ClothCol"} after binding on `nc`; a reader living in another module would answer the fail-closed set or empty and the assertion fails loudly. Of the four singletons only `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` has writer (L25185/25190) and reader (L25270) in DIFFERENT functions; the three skeleton caches are read and written inside the single function that declares them global (L24241/24262, L24281/24306, L10079-10087), and `_actor_can_resolve_bone` reaches `_SKELETON_BONES_CACHE` only through the `_actor_skeleton_bone_names()` call (L10070), so production cannot fork them across a split -- only the test-side namespace assignment goes vacuous (the fixture docstring L67-71 confirms 'with no skeleton the helper deliberately returns the conservative answer'). The '39 mutable containers' is 35 by AST (module-level dict/list/set literals + dict/list/set/defaultdict/OrderedDict/deque/Counter calls) + the 4 None singletons. No split exists in the tree (src/ has no nif_physics/nif_weights etc.; 6 `from . import nif_convert` sites in src, all whole-module), so this is a precondition for a hypothetical refactor. The invariant it states (no `global`, no by-value import of a singleton in any sub-module; writer + reader of `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` co-located; rebind via `nc.X = v`) is sound, NEW (no memory/worklog/CHANGELOG entry covers the module-boundary form; BUG-00 itself is FIXED and confirmed in game 2026-08-19, IN_GAME_BUGS L114/L706), and is what F058 (Module 9) already commits to carry. Keep as a reframed MEDIUM precondition folded under F058, with the consequence corrected.

**Fix.** Same invariant, corrected consequence: (1) in any moved code rewrite the four `global X; X = v` sites to `nc.X = v` (identical semantics while the function lives in nif_convert -- same dict); (2) keep `_hdt_xml_bind_piece_source` (writer) and `_read_source_hdt_xml_text_uncached` (reader) in the same module as `convert_nif`'s bind call, or read via `nc._PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT`; (3) repo test: no `ast.Global` and no `from .nif_convert import <UPPER|_*_CACHE|_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT>` in any `src/nif_*.py` sub-module. State plainly that the regression a violation produces is fail-CLOSED over-protection on destination-side collider/soft-body lookups (grafts skipped -> weight rows differ from baseline) with `hdt_xml_unresolved` in the pass log, so the existing `hdt_xml_unresolved` count (33 in the 08-19 live run) is the run-time detector: any rise after a split is the fork. The three skeleton caches need only the test-side fix (namespace assignment reaching the accessor); production cannot fork them because reader == writer function.

**Proof it landed.** Existing suite already detects the fork: tests/test_hdt_xml_source_of_truth.py::test_destination_lookup_recovers_from_the_bound_source asserts == {"ClothCol"} after `nc._PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT = XML` (a moved reader answers the fail-closed set or empty and FAILS); tests/test_smp_structural_relax.py for the skeleton side. Add the negative arm: with the reader stubbed to see None and a declaring NIF, assert the collider set is the FULL shape set (fail-closed) and `hdt_xml_unresolved` was noted -- not empty. Convert proof per rule 5: golden vertex+weight capture/check with PYTHONHASHSEED=1, exe-harness A/B (`exe_parity_convert.py`), `verify_zero_weight_bones.py`, and assert the `hdt_xml_unresolved` occurrence count is unchanged versus the pre-split run (the collider grafts are weight passes; a position diff cannot see them).

**Prior art.** BUG-00 fail-open: FIXED in 3b714ed (`#xml-source-of-truth` + `#hdt-fail-closed`), confirmed in game 2026-08-19 (memory project_hdt_xml_fail_open, IN_GAME_BUGS BUG-00). The module-boundary form is new; F058 already carries the same rewrite and cites this precondition.

### F017 [MEDIUM] `_precreate_custom_bone_chains` builds its bone set (incl. #smp-constraint-bones) under two mute handlers the caller's recorder cannot see; a failure silently rediscards the constraint anchors with no report entry anywhere
`src/nif_convert.py:18266` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens swallowed-failures · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** A failure here (e.g. `_read_source_hdt_xml_text` or `_xml_referenced_bone_names` raising on one XML dialect) silently drops the constraint nodes; the piece's physics chain is then pruned by the hardener and the report blames the NIF for lacking a shape — the operator chases a shape-name mismatch that does not exist. CHANGELOG L583-588 already records 'the chain-node drop that shipped a class of skirts stretched to the floor' as a shipped defect of exactly this shape.

**Evidence.** L18266-18267 `    except Exception:\n        pass` closes the try at L18231 that builds `_allb` from every shape's bones and, at L18255-18263, the XML-referenced constraint bones (`for _xb in _xml_referenced_bone_names(_xt): if _xb in src_nodes and not _actor_can_resolve_bone(_xb): _allb.add(_xb); _xml_must_keep.add(_xb)`), ending `bone_names = sorted(_allb)` L18265. The block's own comment L18240-18244: 'with the chain's parent nodes gone it has nothing to hang from and the garment free-falls or stretches to the origin'. The caller L2351-2354 wraps the call in `_note_pass_failure("_precreate_custom_bone_chains", _pe)` — unreachable for anything inside this try. Downstream, `_harden_hdt_xml_for_fsmp` L24594-24603 records `hdt_xml_shape_dropped ... shape block(s) pruned because the converted NIF has no such shape`.

**Verifier.** The swallow is real and invisible to every existing channel; the example raiser and the misattribution claim are both refuted, so severity drops to medium. Confirmed: L18232-18267 in `_precreate_custom_bone_chains` is one try (`_allb = set(bone_names)` ... `bone_names = sorted(_allb)`) closed by `except Exception:\n        pass` at L18266-18267, with the #smp-constraint-bones XML seeding L18255-18264 under its own mute handler; the caller `_install_skin` (def L2330) wraps the call at L2351-2354 with `_note_pass_failure("_precreate_custom_bone_chains", _pe)`, which only sees exceptions that ESCAPE, so nothing inside these two tries is ever recorded. AST reproduction of tests/test_no_silent_pass_failures.py's walk: 0 reported, L18266/L18263 absent (guarded calls {Path, _actor_can_resolve_bone, _read_source_hdt_xml_text, _xml_referenced_bone_names, add, getattr, set, sorted} match no PASS_HINT). On failure `_xml_must_keep` stays empty and `custom = [b for b in bone_names if not _is_skeleton_bone(b) or b in _xml_must_keep]` (L18274-18275) rediscards the constraint anchors -- exactly the class IN_GAME_BUGS L3927-3945 and CHANGELOG L487-520 recorded. REFUTED (1): 'raising on one XML dialect' -- `_read_source_hdt_xml_text_uncached` ends `except Exception:\n        return None` (L25299-25300) and voices `hdt_xml_unresolved` / `hdt_xml_sanitised` itself; `_xml_referenced_bone_names` (L10095-10115) is three `re.findall` on a str; `_actor_can_resolve_bone` (L10044) guards its skeleton load. I found no plausible raiser inside the inner try; the outer try's only unguarded calls are pynifly `src_nif.shapes` / `_sh.bone_names`. REFUTED (2): 'the hardener reports the fallout with the wrong root cause' -- `_harden_hdt_xml_for_fsmp` L24508-24603 prunes only `<per-(triangle|vertex)-shape name=>` blocks whose NAME is not in `nif_shapes` and `<weight-threshold bone=>` lines; its docstring L24522-24525 says `<bone>` definitions and `<generic-constraint>` blocks are left untouched. A lost constraint NODE therefore produces no `hdt_xml_shape_dropped` entry at all. Where the fallout actually surfaces: `validate_armor_hdt_xml` warnings (L6141-6165; checks declared `<bone name>` only) or a SILENT XML regeneration via `_source_hdt_needs_missing_chain_bones` (L23561-23610; also `<bone name>` only, so constraint-only bodyA/bodyB bones are invisible to it) at L7463 / L30110 -- and in game as the origin-placed anchor of IN_GAME_BUGS L3927-3932. So the finding's substance (a chain-critical producer that cannot report its own failure, on a class that has shipped twice) stands; its example and its downstream-attribution story do not. Second caller L7399-7402 is voiced via `failed.append(("pelvis-reanchor", repr(_pe)))` but likewise sees only escaped exceptions.

**Fix.** Voice both levels inside the function: L18263 `except Exception as _e: _note_pass_failure("_precreate_custom_bone_chains/xml-bones", _e)` and L18266 `except Exception as _e: _note_pass_failure("_precreate_custom_bone_chains/bone-set", _e)`; keep the caller's wrapper. Drop the 'hardener blames a shape' framing: the fallout is NOT reported by `_harden_hdt_xml_for_fsmp` at all (it leaves `<bone>`/`<generic-constraint>` untouched) -- that is the argument FOR voicing here, since no downstream channel sees a lost constraint-only node (`_source_hdt_needs_missing_chain_bones` and `validate_armor_hdt_xml` both harvest `<bone name>` only). Optional and separate: `_read_source_hdt_xml_text_uncached`'s terminal `except Exception: return None` (L25299-25300) is the only remaining route by which a real XML read error reaches this code as a quiet None; voice it with `hdt_xml_read_failed` if the owner wants the dialect case covered.

**Proof it landed.** Handler-only, exception-path-only; no vertex/weight/bone/XML/plugin byte can change (report text only). Parity: single-harness `exe_parity_convert.py` A/B (rule 5, PYTHONHASHSEED=1 for source runs). Raise-injection: monkeypatch `nc._xml_referenced_bone_names` (or `nc._read_source_hdt_xml_text`) to raise, call `_precreate_custom_bone_chains` on a synthetic NIF whose `filepath` is set, assert `_piece_pass_failures()` contains `_precreate_custom_bone_chains/xml-bones` -- today it returns normally with nothing recorded; second case monkeypatch the fake nif's `shapes` property to raise, assert `/bone-set` recorded.

**Prior art.** New as a handler; the CLASS it would hide is known-open history (IN_GAME_BUGS L3925-3950 'LArmA 01' anchors, CHANGELOG L487-520 chain-node drop, project_chain_nodes_dropped_daf17a9.md) and the 08-22 silent-handler census (test_no_silent_pass_failures.py) is PASS_HINTS-scoped so it never counted this try. Not tried-and-killed: 1580651 voiced the CALLER only.

### F018 [MEDIUM] The overlay-band morph-sync in `generate_armor_tri` is an 83-line `except Exception: pass` outside every recorder AND outside the AST pin (which scans nif_convert.py only); a mid-loop failure ships a MIXED TRI silently
`src/sliderset_gen.py:409` · effort S · risk none · lens swallowed-failures · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** The block's own comment L303-306 describes the defect it prevents: a thin band 'must morph in lockstep with that layer; otherwise it re-sinks under body sliders'. A NameError or scipy change inside it makes every qualifying band re-sink on every preset, the TRI is written, the run is green. This is BUG-01-adjacent morph behaviour that the pose harness cannot see and that only shows as 'preset-dependent clip'.

**Evidence.** sliderset_gen.py L409-410 `    except Exception:\n        pass` closes the try at L328 (`from scipy.spatial import cKDTree as _cKDTree`) that contains the entire overlay-band sync ending `shape_morphs[A] = new_morphs` L407. No flag gates the try; `_BODY_MOTION_MATCH` (L106) gates only one `continue` at L339. `grep -n "_note_pass_failure\|print(\|warn" src/sliderset_gen.py` returns nothing. The callers voice only around the whole TRI build: nif_convert.py L7650-7654 `failed.append(("auto-TRI", f"body-morph unavailable: {_e_tri!r}"))` and L30211-30214 `result_reason = ... + f"auto-TRI generation failed: {e!r}"` — but the TRI is still written with the un-synced morphs, so neither fires.

**Verifier.** CONFIRMED at the cited lines: src/sliderset_gen.py L328 `try:` ... L409-410 `except Exception:\n        pass` closes the whole overlay-band sync (L328-410 = 83 lines, count verified), no flag gates the try, `_BODY_MOTION_MATCH` only gates the `continue` at L338-339, and `grep -nE "_note_pass_failure|print\(|warn|logging|logger|_report|sys\.stderr" src/sliderset_gen.py` returns NOTHING. All three callers (not two: nif_convert.py L7640 phase-1, L9607 `_refresh_armor_tri_after_reimport`, L30201 phase-2) only catch exceptions that ESCAPE `generate_armor_tri`, so the TRI is saved (`atomic_tri_save`) with whatever the block left. The structural guard tests/test_no_silent_pass_failures.py parses ONLY `inspect.getfile(nc)` (nif_convert.py) and the 08-22 census hint list cannot match this block's calls (`_cKDTree`, `query`, `TriMorph`) -- so this handler sits outside both the runtime recorder and the AST pin. CORRECTIONS: (1) the 'scipy change' vector is not real -- `cKDTree` is imported at module top L43 and used at L178 before the block, so the re-import at L329 cannot fail independently; the only vector is a latent code error, and I found no live one (an empty shape does NOT raise: scipy 1.15.3 `cKDTree(np.empty((0,3)))` and an empty query both return cleanly). Severity therefore MEDIUM (latent, and tests/test_overlay_band_morph.py pins the happy-path merge), not high. (2) The failure mode is not a clean 'revert': `shape_morphs[A] = new_morphs` (L406-407) is assigned per band INSIDE the loop, so an exception on the Nth band ships a MIXED TRI -- bands before it synced, the rest un-synced. (3) 'the two `except Exception` bare imports' do not exist: `grep -n except src/sliderset_gen.py` gives exactly one handler, L409. Effort S, not M: nif_convert imports sliderset_gen lazily inside functions (L7625/L9574/L30168), so a lazy `from .nif_convert import _note_pass_failure` inside the handler (wrapped in try for standalone use) has no circularity; the module-level hook is also fine.

**Fix.** In the L409 handler: `except Exception as _e: try: from .nif_convert import _note_pass_failure; _note_pass_failure("generate_armor_tri/overlay-band-sync", _e) except Exception: pass` (nif_convert is already imported by every caller, so no circularity; the try keeps standalone/test use working). Optionally extend tests/test_no_silent_pass_failures.py `_tree()` to also parse sliderset_gen.py so the pin covers the module. Drop the 'two bare imports' item -- there is only one handler in the module.

**Proof it landed.** Handler-only. TRI bytes identical on the golden set (`scripts/golden_output.py`) and vertex parity via single-harness `exe_parity_convert.py` A/B (rule 5). Raise-injection: monkeypatch `sliderset_gen._cKDTree`-path is wrong (it is re-imported inside the try); instead monkeypatch `scipy.spatial.cKDTree.query` to raise on the 2nd call and assert `nc._piece_pass_failures()` contains the label and that bands processed before the raise are NOT synced back (documents the mixed-state behaviour).

**Prior art.** new -- the 08-22 census (IN_GAME_BUGS.md L3385-3391) counted src/ but its hint list cannot match this block, and the pin parses nif_convert.py only.

(2) The 'NameError inside a silent handler' scenario is wrong on both counts: the handler at L29218-29219 is not silent (it appends to `failed`, surfaced at L30137-30141 as `result_reason = "errors during shape copy: ..."`), and an exception there would DROP the whole softcloth push, making the arm differ MORE from control, not read identical. The real test gap stands: nothing exercises the L5542 branch. (3) The unreachability IS already recorded: memory project_current_state.md L134-136 'This rebuild carries it (DEFAULT OFF, no GUI row, so unreachable from a real run -- the same pattern as #edge-scaled-reach)'. What the finding adds and prior art lacks: the missing test, the missing CHANGELOG paragraph, the contrast with three opt-ins wired in the same diff, and that committing it as-is violates the repo's own rule (docs/PIPELINE.md L455-456: 'an opt-in intended to ship gets a Setting entry in the same commit, or it does not get written'). (4) Scope overclaim: memory project_chest_overstandoff_softcloth says softcloth reaches only 2 of 7 flagged world pieces and 'the other 5 are NOT cloth and their cause is UNTRACED' -- this is the fix for a sub-class, not 'the' fix for the defect; the defect itself has NO IN_GAME_BUGS.md row (grep 'too far' hits only BUG-08). Severity high -> medium.

**Fix.** Add `Setting("softcloth_own_plane", ..., default=False, env="CBBE2UBE_SOFTCLOTH_OWN_PLANE", invert=False, advanced=True)` under Armor / Fit and clearance, tooltip carrying the header's 2.186u->1.800u measurement AND the memory's caveat that the trimmed clearance is jiggle headroom on physics cloth; add tests/test_softcloth_own_plane.py against `_inflate_cloth_over_bust_butt` directly (synthetic poke: a vert with own_clear >= clear is skipped; need is capped to clear - own_clear when ON; OFF reproduces the existing 4 tests bit-for-bit); add the CHANGELOG 'Added (off by default)' paragraph naming the class as chain-driven bust cloth (>= 0.20 breast-band chain weight), not 'chest too far off' as a whole. Do this BEFORE the commit, per PIPELINE.md L455-456. If the owner instead keeps it unreachable, say so in the L5430-5455 header the way L21384-21387 does.

**Proof it landed.** Row round trip as tests/test_promoted_defaults.py: ticked emits CBBE2UBE_SOFTCLOTH_OWN_PLANE=1, unticked emits NOTHING (env_string_for returns None at default, so no converted byte moves while off). Liveness via exe_parity_convert.py on ONE chain-driven bust piece (the 25%-chain ivory class from the memory), ON vs control in one harness, both weights, PYTHONHASHSEED=1 if source; assert the vertex set DIFFERS and that `result_reason` is empty (an 'errors during shape copy' line means the arm never ran). The in-game verdict must be taken UNDER A BODY PRESET, not zero sliders -- the memory shows BIND clip 0.000 in every arm, so a default-body look cannot distinguish them -- and must be judged on jiggle, which no bind-pose metric here can score. Verdict before commit (feedback_verify_in_game_before_commit).

**Prior art.** project_current_state.md L134-136 already records 'DEFAULT OFF, no GUI row, so unreachable from a real run'; project_chest_overstandoff_softcloth.md records it 'IN THE TREE, DEFAULT OFF' and that it reaches 2 of 7 flagged pieces; docs/PIPELINE.md L455-456 is the rule it will breach at commit.

**Claim.** An OPEN defect's stated resolution path requires a configuration the user physically cannot run, so the bug cannot close by its own recipe; and the bug log and the flag audit disagree about whether one of the two flags should ever be judged. Neither env appears in gui_settings.py (93 env rows enumerated). IN_GAME_BUGS.md L125 BUG-08 row: 'OPEN - best arm found (3.116 -> 2.621, all 4 criteria), unjudged | judge `#derived-clearance` + `#panel-local-rigid` in game'. IN_GAME_BUGS.md L2145 'ATTEMPT 3 - `#panel-local-rigid`: WORKS, opt-in, VERDICT PENDING'. project_current_state L1595 '(opt-in, DEFAULT OFF, no verdict)'. project_flag_audit_2026_08_18: 'Deliberately unreachable, KEEP OFF: DERIVED_CLEARANCE (indicted twice)'.

**Verifier.** CONFIRMED unreachability and gate walk: L26247 `_PANEL_LOCAL_RIGID = _flag("CBBE2UBE_PANEL_LOCAL_RIGID", False)`, L26248 `_PANEL_LOCAL_K = int(_knob("CBBE2UBE_PANEL_LOCAL_K", 64.0))`; grep gives exactly 2 further refs, both inside `_partial_rigid_panels` (L26370 `if _PANEL_LOCAL_RIGID:`, L26375 `_locally_rigid_panel(P, Q, _PANEL_LOCAL_K)`), so K is inert unless the flag is set. `_partial_rigid_panels` (def L26325) is called at four sites (L6746, L7161, L28529, L28950), each in the `else` of `PANEL_RIGID_EARLY_CLEAR` (default False, L26194) with PANEL_RIGIDITY=0.75 (L26122) -> reachable on BOTH paths at defaults once the env is set. It is NOT consulted by `_rigidify_within_clearance` (def L26469; post-antipoke site L29162), which is where PANEL_RIGID_SURFACE_GUARD acts (L26520). L1396 `DERIVED_CLEARANCE_TARGET = _flag("CBBE2UBE_DERIVED_CLEARANCE", False)`, consumed at L4001 in conform_to_source_standoff and fed by `_diff_conf` (L28862, computed regardless of the flag) at L28875 and `_antipoke_diff` at L29112 -> reachable when set. `grep -c 'env="' src/gui_settings.py` = 93, zero hits for PANEL_LOCAL or DERIVED_CLEARANCE. IN_GAME_BUGS.md L125 row and L2145/L2179 verified verbatim; git log -S: _PANEL_LOCAL_RIGID first in 3b714ed 2026-08-19, DERIVED_CLEARANCE_TARGET in f0580f5 2026-08-15 (both committed). CORRECTIONS: (1) 'the contradiction is not recorded anywhere' is FALSE -- the bug log records the tension itself: L2191-2199 (ATTEMPT 4) calls derived-clearance 'INDICTED TWICE' and L2310-2320 re-measures the nipple-headroom indictment on the suit ('Headroom is UNCHANGED to slightly BETTER on every derived arm ... ONE PIECE, not a refutation of the class ... should be looked at in game rather than assumed lost'). So the OPEN row is a dated-but-reasoned call, not an oversight; what is stale is the 08-18 flag audit's 'KEEP OFF' which predates that 08-19 re-measurement and was never updated (its 08-26 DELTA section is silent on it). The folding indictment (428/47 -> 652/109 on a fine-tessellated strap piece, project_derived_clearance_target) is NOT rebutted anywhere and is a pack-class risk a one-suit in-game look cannot clear (rule 2). (2) Proof correction: arm d's 3.116 -> 2.621 was measured 08-19, BEFORE #panel-rigidity was wired into both paths (08-22), before bust_morph_chord and panel_rigid_surface_guard went default-ON (08-26, uncommitted -- HEAD has 0 hits for PANEL_RIGID_SURFACE_GUARD), and the surface guard acts in the post-antipoke rigidity half that IS the 'canceller' this bug is about; asserting the old numbers reproduce is unsound -- re-measure the control on the current build and pre-register the four criteria again. (3) Do NOT expose the K knob: the log (L2330+) and project_edge_scaled_reach L85-90 record K as 'not a monotone dial and is exhausted' (K=32/16 fail both fold criteria; only 64 and 96 pass). (d) Update project_flag_audit_2026_08_18's DELTA with the 08-19 headroom re-measurement so the two documents stop disagreeing. In-game verdict under a preset before any default moves.

### F026 [MEDIUM] Three KNOWN_PHASE2_ONLY reasons (bake_preset, rebury, bust-plate sync) are plumbing claims the code contradicts; one is design, one is unreachable, one is opt-in-only true
`tests/test_convert_path_parity.py:172` · effort S · risk none · lens two-path-drift · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** The guard checks that a reason EXISTS, not that it is TRUE (the file says so itself, and 4 such reasons were already wrong for 4 days). Three more entries are structural-sounding claims that are false against the code; the honest label for all three is the same DEBT/design-question the ride entries now carry. Anyone sizing BUG-02 from this list under-counts the copy path's reachable clearance work by three passes.

**Evidence.** tests L172: `"bake_preset_into_armor": "bakes the preset onto the injected body"` - but nif_convert L28316-28327 reads `_find_ube_template_body()` and `_find_user_preset_body(weight)` from DISK and L28752-28756 calls `bake_preset_into_armor(_sv_body, preset_template_verts, preset_user_verts, k=4, close_threshold=5.0)`; the signature (L8168-8175) takes no body shape - it moves ARMOR verts by a preset delta and never touches `ube_nif`/`body_verts_for_p2`. tests L169: `"rebury_authored_verts": "needs BOTH source and UBE body..."` - the copy path has both in scope: `cbbe_verts_for_warp` + `_cached_cbbe_body_normals(cbbe_body_path_p1)` (L6968) + `body_verts_for_fit`/`body_normals_for_fit`, the exact pair `#authored-inflate` already consumes there (L6979-6981, comment L6953-6957 'it does have the body the garment was authored against'). tests L185: `"_sync_bust_plate_follow_postwrite": "post-write, needs the injected body"` - the pass takes only `dst_path` (L20264) and explicitly SKIPS BaseShape (L20345 `if s.name == "BaseShape" or s.name in RESKIN_SKIP_NAMES:`); it pairs cloth layers to each other, not to a body.

**Verifier.** CONFIRMED on the letter for all three entries: none of the three reasons describes what the code does. (1) bake_preset: L28316-28317 `tmpl_p = _find_ube_template_body()` / `user_p = _find_user_preset_body(weight)` load two bodies from DISK; L28752-28756 `bake_preset_into_armor(_sv_body, preset_template_verts, preset_user_verts, k=4, close_threshold=5.0)`; the signature L8168-8175 takes armor_verts + two body arrays and nothing from `ube_nif`/`body_verts_for_p2`. The copy path already reaches `_find_user_preset_body` (walker: copy=True). (2) rebury: signature L3778-3782 needs (src_cloth, src_body_verts, src_body_normals, cur_cloth, ube_body_verts, ube_body_normals, tris, body_motion_weight); the copy path binds `sv_world`, `cbbe_verts_for_warp`, `_a_bn = _cached_cbbe_body_normals(cbbe_body_path_p1)` (L6966-6970), `snapped`, `body_verts_for_fit`, `body_normals_for_fit`, `s.tris` at L6972-6983 -- the pair `#authored-inflate` consumes, comment L6955-6957 'it does have the body the garment was authored against'. (3) bust-plate sync: `def _sync_bust_plate_follow_postwrite(dst_path) -> int` (L20264); L20345 `if s.name == "BaseShape" or s.name in RESKIN_SKIP_NAMES: continue`; it pairs breast-weighted cloth layers by overlap. The copy path's tail at L7671-7677 calls `_finalize_physics_and_motion_match(dst_path, ...)` then `_cap_weight_roughness_to_author(dst_path, ...)` with the very `dst_path` -- the sync call (phase 2 L30257, between those same two) is simply absent there. Walker re-run (tests' own `_call_graph`/`_reachable`, stop={convert_nif_phase2}): all three copy=False phase2=True; phase2-only set is now 10 names (55/65), not the 12 the memory cites. The test itself admits the limit: L445-446 'A reason here is a CLAIM -- four were false for four days'. THREE CORRECTIONS the finding needs: (a) rebury is NOT 'reachable clearance work' on any path: `REBURY_AUTHORED = _flag("CBBE2UBE_REBURY_AUTHORED", False)` (L3751), no Setting(...) row in gui_settings.py (rg 'rebury' = 0 hits), and it was KILLED in game (PIPELINE.md L493-497: 3132 shoulder verts) -- so the under-count is two passes, not three; also `body_motion_weight` comes from `_body_breast_motion_weight(ube_base_for_pass1)` (L28362), a body SHAPE with bone weights, which the copy path's `_cached_ube_body_verts` binding does not obviously supply (None is the documented bad behaviour, L3792-3793). (b) bust-plate sync's reason is true for ONE sub-branch: the opt-in `BUST_PLATE_CHAIN_TRANSPLANT` (L19331 default False, GUI-reachable via Setting 'bust_plate_chain_transplant' L1048) calls `_breast_chain_levers(nf)` (L20429-20430) which reads `BaseShape` and returns [] without it (L20231-20233) -- on a copy-path NIF (no injection) the transplant is inert while the default core still works. Honest reason: 'core is body-blind; only the opt-in chain transplant reads the injected BaseShape'. (c) bake_preset's honest label is a DESIGN question, not only plumbing: the injected body is deliberately the un-morphed template (auto_convert.py L439-461 'preferring sources that DON'T have the user's BodySlide preset baked into them'), whereas BOTH paths' warp target `_find_ube_femalebody` prefers the user's preset-built body (L2046-2049 `real = _find_user_preset_body(weight)`), so wiring bake_preset into the copy path would stack the preset delta on a body that already carries it (and phase 2 may already do exactly that: bake at L28752 then warp toward the preset body at L28373 -- out of this finding's scope, worth its own line). fit_armor retirement: `fit_armor=` is passed by no caller (rg src/ tools/ tests/ = 0 hits; default False L28004), so 'the body-swap fit itself' describes a dead call -- already recorded (IN_GAME_BUGS.md L1614-1617, PIPELINE.md L60), relabel is fine and the entry must stay listed because the call site keeps it phase2-only by call-graph (test L237-241 rejects stale entries). Severity: medium -- test/doc-only; the harm is mis-sizing BUG-02, not a mesh byte.

**Fix.** Rewrite the three entries to what is TRUE per call site: bake_preset -> 'DESIGN QUESTION, not plumbing: takes armor verts + two on-disk bodies (L28316, L28752); the copy path has both. NOT to be wired in by reasoning: the injected body is the slider-zero template (auto_convert L439-461) while both paths warp toward the user-preset body (L2047), so a copy-path bake would stack the preset twice -- needs a morph A/B'; rebury -> 'DEBT but UNREACHABLE on both paths (REBURY_AUTHORED default OFF, no Setting row, killed in game PIPELINE L493); copy path has 6 of 7 inputs (L6966-6983), lacks a body shape for _body_breast_motion_weight'; bust-plate sync -> 'core is body-blind and takes only dst_path (L20264, skips BaseShape L20345); the copy path tail at L7671-7677 has that dst_path; only the opt-in BUST_PLATE_CHAIN_TRANSPLANT branch reads the injected BaseShape (L20429, L20231)'; fit_armor -> 'DEAD on both paths: fit_armor=False, no caller passes it'. Pin machine-checkably where cheap: assert bake_preset_into_armor's call carries no body-shape argument and _sync_bust_plate_follow_postwrite's signature is (dst_path) only. Do NOT wire any of the three into the copy path off this finding.

**Proof it landed.** Test/doc-only. Run tests/test_convert_path_parity.py (entries stay phase2-only by call-graph, so test_phase2_only_passes_are_the_known_documented_set stays green). No convert needed for the rewrite; any later wiring is a behaviour change on ~74% of the pack and needs the BUG-02 pack A/B + clearance counter-metric, plus a morph A/B for bake_preset specifically.

**Prior art.** Partial prior art: memory project_convert_path_divergence.md and IN_GAME_BUGS.md L1605-1623 record the FOUR layer-ride reasons as false and list these three as 'the genuine prize' without checking their reasons; fit_armor dead-on-both-paths is recorded there and in docs/PIPELINE.md L60. The three-reasons-false point itself is new; the reasons date from 87daf2a (2026-08-18) and no later commit touched them.

**Claim.** §2b's own load-bearing rule two paragraphs later (:95 'WHOEVER CREATES A NODE FIRST WINS, and the chain writer needs its PARENT') makes creation order the thing a maintainer must get right; the list puts the node-creating pass first when it actually runs last, per shape, at write time, after every weight map has been computed. Someone adding a bone-dependent weight pass 'after precreate, per §2b' would put it before the nodes exist.

**Evidence.** PIPELINE.md:86-90: "`_precreate_custom_bone_chains` (chain nodes; ...) → `add_scale_bone_weights` → `compute_body_blend_skinning` (the \"M6 reskin\") → `_slot_aware_*` band/reach → `_sync_chest_layered_cloth_weights` / `_sync_abdomen_...`". Code: `_precreate_custom_bone_chains(` has 3 hits in nif_convert.py — the def (18206), a direct copy-path call at 7399 gated on `if _has_nif_root_garment_chain(src_nif_for_fit):` (7391), and 2352 inside `_install_skin` ("new_shape.skin()\n    # Preserve physics-bone chains BEFORE add_bone ...\n        _precreate_custom_bone_chains(dst_nif, src_shape.file, bone_names)"), which is reached from `_copy_shape` at 29979 (phase 2) / 7414-7419 (copy path). PASS_MAP.md's `convert_nif_phase2` table (208-382) has NO `_precreate_custom_bone_chains` row. On phase 2 `compute_body_blend_skinning` is at 29582 and `add_scale_bone_weights` at 29609 ("bones, xforms_map, weights_map = compute_body_blend_skinning(" then "bones, xforms_map, weights_map = add_scale_bone_weights("); the syncs at 29758/29763 and the write at 29979 come after both. Copy path: reskin 7206, scale 7221, syncs 7333/7338, precreate 7399, `_copy_shape` 7414.

**Verifier.** PIPELINE.md:86-90 puts `_precreate_custom_bone_chains` first and `add_scale_bone_weights` before `compute_body_blend_skinning`. Code: precreate is called inside `_install_skin` at :2352 (write time, per shape) plus one gated copy-path direct call at :7399; on both paths the reskin precedes the scale-bone graft (copy 7206 -> 7221, phase 2 29582 -> 29609). PASS_MAP's only `_precreate_custom_bone_chains` row (line 163) is in the `convert_nif` table, none in the phase-2 table. Doc-only fix.

**Fix.** Rewrite §2b as two lanes: (1) weight maps computed in memory in this order — reskin (`compute_body_blend_skinning`) → scale-bone graft (`add_scale_bone_weights`) → slot band/reach → the two syncs; (2) node creation at write time — `_seed_flat_chain_anchors` first into the empty NIF (28086), then per shape inside `_install_skin`: `_precreate_custom_bone_chains` → STB align → strip/fill → `add_bone`. Note the copy path's extra early call at 7399 and its gate.

**Proof it landed.** Doc-only. Verify with the enclosing-function AST probe used here (line 2352 → `_install_skin`; 29582 < 29609 < 29758 < 29979) and PASS_MAP's phase-2 table having no precreate row. No vertex comparison needed.

**Prior art.** Not found in the memory index or worklog.

### F039 [MEDIUM] docs/TOOL_MAP.md `gate` and `floor` columns are inferred by shallow AST/keyword rules and are wrong in both directions
`scripts/tool_map.py:234` · effort M · risk none · lens harness-hygiene · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-open, fix=sound, confidence=high

**Claim.** The index the standing rule ('check TOOL_MAP before hand-rolling a probe') depends on tells a reader that six non-gating tools can gate a run and that a tool with a known 0/0 hole has a floor. This is the same 'detector that reports zero is the kind most likely broken' class the memory records for comment_audit, and its own test can only fail in one direction.

**Evidence.** tool_map.py:234-244 `_has_floor`: `probes = ("measured NOTHING", ..., "empty set", ...)` / `return any(p in text for p in probes)` — a substring search over the whole file. golden_output.py:51 matches on prose ('returns an empty set') and is rendered `floor: yes` although check() has no floor (previous finding). tool_map.py:207-231 `_exits` collects EVERY `sys.exit(N)`/main `return N`; TOOL_MAP.md header says 'a tool with one can be chained into a gate', yet verify_bodymatch.py:161, verify_bust_clearance.py:235, verify_layered_cloth.py:116, verify_motion_match.py:162, find_morph_follow_gaps.py:138, find_overinflation.py:120 all end `return 0` unconditionally — their only `return 1` is 'output not found', so `gate: 1` is a misconfiguration abort, not a verdict. tests/test_tool_map.py:186-194 `test_the_floor_detector_finds_the_known_cases` asserts three known positives and has no negative control.

**Verifier.** Evidence verified at the cited lines: tool_map.py:234-244 `_has_floor` is a substring probe (`"empty set"` in the tuple) and `_exits` (207-231) collects every sys.exit/main-return constant. golden_output.py:51 is prose ('returns an empty set') yet TOOL_MAP.md:52 renders `scripts/golden_output.py | 2 | yes`. All six named verify/find tools have exactly one `return 1` (the 'output not found' abort, e.g. verify_bodymatch.py:115-117) and an unconditional `return 0`, yet TOOL_MAP.md:25/44 show gate `1` under a header (L11) that says such a tool 'can be chained into a gate'. test_tool_map.py:186-194 has positive cases only. Prior art: project_scratchpad_tooling.md admits floor is a keyword lower bound; the gate-column abort-vs-verdict conflation is unrecorded. Fix is docs/tooling only; risk none is honest. Not in phase1_settled.json.

**Fix.** Split `gate` into verdict codes vs abort codes by requiring an explicit `EXIT:` block in each tool's docstring (e.g. `EXIT: 1 = regression found; 2 = misconfigured; 3 = measured nothing`) and rendering that instead of AST-guessing; derive `floor` from the presence of a `3 = measured nothing` line or of the shared `require_population` call, not prose. Add a negative fixture (a tool whose docstring says 'empty set' but never checks) to test_tool_map.py and to tool_audit.py's HAS_FLOOR calibration.

**Proof it landed.** Regenerate with `python scripts/tool_map.py`; assert golden_output.py now renders `floor: —` until its floor is added, and the six verify/find tools render `abort: 1` with no verdict code; `python scripts/tool_map.py --check` and `pytest tests/test_tool_map.py` pass with the new negative test; docstring contract test fails today on the 29 tools below and passes after.

**Prior art.** project_scratchpad_tooling.md (floor column = keyword detector, lower bound) — gate-column conflation is new.

### F042 [MEDIUM] The deploy gate script pipes the regression check through `| tail`, so a regression can never set `fail`; six scratchpad A/B .ps1 drivers pipe the convert to Out-Null with no $LASTEXITCODE check
`scratchpad_handoff_2026_08_13/gate_all.sh:12` · effort S · risk none · lens harness-hygiene · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-open, fix=sound, confidence=high

**Claim.** The named deploy gate fails OPEN on the exact condition it exists for (it fails closed only on a MISSING candidate). The memory records the `| tail` trap as a lesson learned but the script that taught it was never fixed. The .ps1 drivers reproduce the 'diff of two empty captures reports identical' class.

**Evidence.** gate_all.sh:12 `python regression_check.py "$B/$1" "$CAND/$1" 2>&1 | tail -12 || fail=1` under `#!/bin/sh` (L1, no pipefail) — `||` sees tail's status, so regression_check.py's `sys.exit(1)` (regression_check.py:98-101 'REGRESSION -- do not deploy') never reaches `fail`; the header L3 promises 'Exits non-zero if any piece regressed' and L21 prints 'DO NOT DEPLOY' only on `fail`. L5 `SC=".../961c2205-a199-4141-8206-e5a593e49b8b/scratchpad"` — that session dir no longer exists (ls: No such file), so the default candidate root is dead. PowerShell: ab_pieces.ps1:22-23 `python scripts\convert_one_armor.py ... $out | Out-Null` and the same line in ab_conform_pop.ps1:38, bisect_writetime.ps1:34, fidelity_ablation.ps1:46, floor_sweep.ps1:41, twin_guard_safety.ps1:39 — grep count of `LASTEXITCODE` in all six: 0, of `$?`: 0 — a failed convert leaves a stale or absent arm and the next line compares it. Tracked scripts: 0 `| tail`/`| head`; build_exe.ps1:65-66 and deploy_exe.ps1:52-55 do check status.

**Verifier.** gate_all.sh read whole: `#!/bin/sh`, no `set -o pipefail`, L12 `python regression_check.py ... 2>&1 | tail -12 || fail=1` — `||` tests tail's status, so regression_check.py:98-101 (`REGRESSION -- do not deploy` then `sys.exit(1)`) cannot set `fail`; only the `[ ! -f ]` MISSING branch does. L5 session dir 961c2205-... does not exist (ls confirmed), so the default candidate root is dead. ab_pieces.ps1:22-23 pipes convert_one_armor.py to Out-Null; grep of LASTEXITCODE/`$?` in all six .ps1 = 0 each. feedback_method_traps.md:353 records the `| tail` trap generically but the script still has it. phase1_settled.json already notes tracked scripts have 0 pipe-to-tail — this finding is scratchpad-only, consistent with that. Fix is harness-only, risk none honest.

**Fix.** gate_all.sh: `out=$(python regression_check.py ... 2>&1); rc=$?; printf '%s\n' "$out" | tail -12; [ $rc -ne 0 ] && fail=1`, and require the candidate root as an argument instead of a dead session path. Each .ps1: `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "convert failed: $stem/$arm" }` after the convert, and `Test-Path` the arm NIF before the compare. Better: retire the six .ps1 drivers in favour of survival_sweep.py's subprocess pattern (checks returncode AND the shape-copy string).

**Proof it landed.** Point gate_all.sh at a candidate dir containing one piece known to regress (or make regression_check exit 1 via a synthetic swap of baseline/candidate) and assert the script prints 'DO NOT DEPLOY' and exits 1 (today: 'ALL PIECES CLEAN', exit 0). For a .ps1: give convert_one_armor.py an unresolvable slot (it exits 2) and assert the driver throws before the compare.

**Prior art.** feedback_method_traps.md:353-355 (`| tail` throws away the exit code) — the named gate still carrying it is unrecorded.

### F043 [MEDIUM] Three scratchpad A/B harnesses still clear os.environ before building the arm env; only reach_ab.py was fixed
`scratchpad_handoff_2026_08_13/derived_scoped_ab.py:70` · effort S · risk none · lens harness-hygiene · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-open, fix=sound, confidence=high

**Claim.** The `_env()` in each reads an already-empty environ, so the arm runs with PATH/SYSTEMROOT gone and (per the memory) dies only sometimes on a lazy numpy.random import. These are the panel-rigidity and derived-clearance A/B harnesses the bug log cites; an arm that dies late can leave a partial output that the compare step reads as an arm.

**Evidence.** derived_scoped_ab.py:70-71 `os.environ.clear()` / `os.environ.update(_env(extra))`; panel_clear_cap_ab.py:56-57 and panel_similarity_reseat_ab.py:79-80 identical. reach_ab.py:150-159 carries the fix and the diagnosis ('BUILD THE ENV BEFORE CLEARING IT ... Inherited from derived_scoped_ab.py and panel_similarity_reseat_ab.py, which both still have it') — naming two of the three. grep 'environ.clear' over scripts/, tests/, src/: 0 hits; the tracked tools use `env = dict(os.environ); env.update(env_extra)` + subprocess (coherence_repair_ab.py:119-120, inflate_census.py:342-344), which cannot exhibit the bug.

**Verifier.** Repo-wide grep (excluding dist/build/pynifly): exactly 5 `environ.clear` hits, all scratchpad — derived_scoped_ab.py:70, panel_clear_cap_ab.py:56, panel_similarity_reseat_ab.py:79 in the buggy `clear(); update(_env(extra))` order, and reach_ab.py:150-160 with the fix and a comment naming only two survivors. derived_scoped_ab.py:70-71 read: `os.environ.clear()` / `os.environ.update(_env(extra))` — confirmed. feedback_method_traps.md:312-318 and IN_GAME_BUGS.md:95-100 record the bug in 'three A/B harnesses' but do not say which nor that they remain unfixed; git log -S environ.clear shows no tracked commit ever touched it. phase1_settled.json confirms 0 hits in tracked scripts. Fix is harness-only; risk none honest.

**Fix.** Fix the three (`env = _env(extra); os.environ.clear(); os.environ.update(env)`), or better delete the in-process re-import pattern (clear + `del sys.modules['src.*']` + re-import) from all four and route them through the subprocess pattern the tracked tools use — in-process flag switching is the producer of this whole class because `_flag()` reads env at import.

**Proof it landed.** Run each harness's `run()` control arm and assert `os.environ["SYSTEMROOT"]` and `PATH` survive the switch; then assert the ON arm's written NIF DIFFERS from the control (memory rule: an arm identical to its control is a broken arm). No src/ change.

**Prior art.** feedback_method_traps.md:312-318 / IN_GAME_BUGS.md:95 record the class; that the three survivors are still unfixed and panel_clear_cap_ab.py is unnamed is new.

### F044 [MEDIUM] 29 of 69 tracked tools have a non-zero exit path their docstring never mentions; 18 gates never say what PASS/FAIL means; tool_audit only checks that a docstring exists
`scripts/tool_audit.py:170` · effort M · risk none · lens harness-hygiene · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=sound, confidence=medium

**Claim.** The 2026-08-26 'Gate: PASS' misread happened because the reader had to infer a gate's contract from its sections; with no stated exit semantics, TOOL_MAP has to guess them from the AST (previous finding) and gets them wrong. 'What PASS means' is the load-bearing sentence for a gate and it is absent from most of them.

**Evidence.** tool_audit.py:170-171 `if not ast.get_docstring(tree):` / `undoc.append(p)` — the only docstring check; all 69 tools pass it. Docstring probe over the 69 TOOL_MAP entries: non-zero exit present but the word 'exit'/'exit code' absent from the docstring: 29 (e.g. armor_clip_diag.py exit 2, audit_sink.py exits 2/3, bust_verdict.py exit 3, fold_census.py exit 2, inflate_census.py exits 2/3, morph_clip_test.py exit 3, posed_clip_test.py exits 1/2, preset_ab_score.py exit 3, stage_morph_ledger.py exit 3, physics_cloth_health.py exit 2, verify_weight_invariant.py exit 1); gate present but no PASS/FAIL/verdict/regress wording: 18 (convert_one_armor, armor_clip_diag, authored_offset_ledger, find_morph_follow_gaps, find_overinflation, multipose_clip_test, nif_semantic_diff, normal_rotation, pass_ledger, physics_cloth_health, preset_ab_score, verify_bodymatch, verify_chain_shift, verify_layered_cloth, verify_motion_match, verify_weight_invariant, registered_bone_audit, survival_sweep); no statement of what is read: 11; no `python ...` usage line: 20 (TOOL_MAP.md 'Invocations' section covers only 49). Counter-example of the contract done right: zero_weight_pair_ab.py:51 'Exit 1 if any bone was newly emptied, 2 if nothing was measured.'

**Verifier.** tool_audit.py:170-171 is the only docstring check (`if not ast.get_docstring(tree): undoc.append(p)`) — confirmed. Spot-checked three of the named tools with an AST probe: bust_verdict.py exits 3 at L235/239/248 with no 'exit' or PASS/FAIL in its docstring; fold_census.py returns 2 at L155/166/179 with no 'exit' in its docstring; verify_weight_invariant.py has a real verdict exit (L112 `return 1 if (tot_over or tot_bad) else 0`) that its docstring never states. Counter-example zero_weight_pair_ab.py:51 confirmed verbatim. The 29/18/11/20 counts were not re-run (triage; medium confidence). phase1_settled.json records tool_audit's deliberate scope (floors on source-parsing tools only) — this finding proposes a new section, not a duplicate. Docs-only fix; risk none honest. Pairs with F039 (the EXIT: block would feed tool_map's gate column).

**Fix.** Require a three-line contract in every tool docstring — `READS: ...`, `PASS MEANS: ...`, `EXIT: 0 = ..., N = ...` — enforce it as tool_audit.py section 4 (exit 1 for TRACKED tools missing it, scratchpad listed only, same policy as sections 1-3), and have tool_map.py render the EXIT line verbatim in the gate column.

**Proof it landed.** `python scripts/tool_audit.py` exits 1 today on the 29/18 lists above and 0 after; `python scripts/tool_map.py --check` current; `pytest tests/test_tool_map.py tests/test_analysis_repo_root.py` green. Docs-only change; no src/ file touched.

**Prior art.** new; complements F039 (same producer: no stated exit contract forces tool_map to guess).

`src/ube_patcher.py:2150` · effort S · risk none · lens io-encoding-determinism · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=high

### F049 [MEDIUM] Module 1 — nif_telemetry.py: pass-failure / change-attribution recorders (leaf, 0 outgoing edges)
`src/nif_convert.py:5732` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens monolith-seams · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=sound, confidence=high

**Claim.** The cleanest seam in the file: a leaf with a stated contract ('NEVER raises', per-piece list reset only at the single entry point). ### F051 [MEDIUM] Module 3 — nif_body_refs.py: body discovery, VFS/mod-dir resolution and the body-derived caches (25 functions; 20 of the 39 mutable containers; a mid-file `import paths` travels with it)
`src/nif_convert.py:1955` · effort M · risk vert-neutral · lens monolith-seams · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=sound, confidence=high

**Claim.** This is the 'decided by its first caller' module: every function memoises into a process-wide dict keyed by env/paths, and the memory records that touching one before the discovered paths are exported locks in a wrong answer for the run. Moving it is safe only if the caches stay in core and are read as `nc._X` (so the four test sites that REPLACE `_BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE` by assignment still empty the dict the accessors use — otherwise those tests pass against a warm cache, i.e. vacuously). `_HDT_DIR_SCAN_CACHE` is dead (0 accessors) and should be deleted, not moved.

**Evidence.** L1933 banner `Portable body discovery (no hardcoded modpack paths)`; L1944 `from . import paths as _paths  # noqa: E402` (an import in the middle of the file); L1955 `_BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE: "dict[str, Path | None]" = {}` (accessors `_find_cbbe_base_body`, `_find_ube_femalebody`, `_find_ube_shapedata`, `_find_user_preset_body`, plus L25370-25404 in `_find_ube_body_osd`); caches L151-161 (`_OSD_CACHE`, `_UBE_BODY_REF_CACHE`, `_BODY_MORPH_AMP_CACHE`, `_SCALE_BONE_DATA_CACHE`, `_HDT_DIR_SCAN_CACHE`, `_CBBE_UBE_DELTA_CACHE`), L1328 `_AUTHORED_SRC_TREE`, L1501, L1765, L1884, L11459-11460, L11593 + L11597 `_BODY_JIGGLE_NF_KEEPALIVE`, L11962, L18874, L23685 `_ube_conform_tree_cache`, L24107-24109 `_MOD_DIR_LIST_CACHE` / `_VFS_DATA_REL_MEMO` / `_GLOB_FIRST_MEMO`. AST: `_HDT_DIR_SCAN_CACHE` (L160) has ZERO accessor functions. Outgoing: 12 names, all leaves (`_pynifly`, `_looks_like_inline_body`, 3 skin-frame, `_vertex_normals_from_tris`, `_is_scale_bone`, `_is_physics_jiggle_scale_bone`, `_closest_point_delta`, `_shape_has_3ba_topology`, `_iter_femalebody_nifs`, `_safe_data_rel`). Called by entry 20, physics 11, fit 6, weights 6. Siblings: auto_convert.py L108-122 warms `_find_ube_body_osd`, `_cached_osd_load`, `_cached_ube_body_verts`, `_find_cbbe_base_body`, `_find_ube_femalebody`, `_cached_cbbe_to_ube_delta` per worker; L4875, L5223; cli/build_mod/preflight use `_find_*`. Tests: 4 sites `monkeypatch.setattr(nc, "_BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE", {})` (test_audit_2026_07_04.py L292/298/308).

**Verifier.** L1933 banner, L1944 mid-file `from . import paths as _paths  # noqa: E402`, L1955 `_BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE` all present. `_HDT_DIR_SCAN_CACHE` (L160) dead-code claim CONFIRMED by full rg over src/tests/scripts: exactly 1 hit, the definition itself; git log -S shows it arrived in the initial commit 756bf53 and was never used. The 'caches stay in core so `monkeypatch.setattr(nc, "_BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE", {})` still empties the dict the accessors use' reasoning is sound (attribute replacement vs in-place). Deleting the dead dict is byte-changing to source but cannot touch a vertex.

**Fix.** Move the 25 functions + the L1944 `paths` import; caches stay in core, accessed as `nc._BODY_DISCOVERY_CACHE[...]` etc.; delete `_HDT_DIR_SCAN_CACHE` (L160) in its own commit after an AST count of 0 references; state the export-paths-before-first-call contract in the module docstring (it is currently only in memory). Re-point: `_glob_first_in_mods` 2 patch sites (2 same-module callers) and `_cached_body_morph_stack` 3 sites (1 same-module caller).

**Proof it landed.** golden capture/check at tolerance 0 with PYTHONHASHSEED=1 AND with the same CBBE2UBE_* body/path env on both arms (golden refuses across a different flag set; a discovery change would surface as a different body reference = whole-pack displacement); pytest node-ID diff; the postrun 'skeleton looks unloaded' refusal from feedback_method_traps still applies to any probe that imports this module early.

**Prior art.** feedback_method_traps 'A CACHED lookup is decided by its FIRST caller' (`_actor_skeleton_bone_names`, `postrun_audit.py` check B); project_converter_output_is_skeleton_dependent; project_bodymatch_source_selection / project_3ba_source_selection_bug (the selection logic that lives here). **Claim.** The project already diagnosed and fixed this idiom in the pass-map generator and wrote the lesson into memory; the parity test that pins the monolith's structure did not get the fix, so the single most important guard for a split is also the slowest thing in the suite — which makes people run it less, exactly when the split needs it run on every commit.
