Measured on this machine: 43.5 s for the 308 calls; the pre-split slice (`lines[a][col:] + lines[a+1:b] + lines[b][:end_col]`) 0.004 s with `bodies == bodies2` True. scripts/pass_map.py L95-108 `_segment()` already implements exactly that with the comment 'NOT `ast.get_source_segment`: that re-splits the WHOLE source on every call, and this module is 1.2 MB. Profiled -- 513 calls cost 150 of the generator's 151 seconds'.

**Verifier.** Confirmed at L343-344: `bodies = {n.name: ast.get_source_segment(src, n) or "" for n in ast.parse(src).body if isinstance(n, ast.FunctionDef)}`; `_mesh_writers(g)` called twice (L363, L402). Timing reproduced: 15 calls = 1.96 s -> ~40 s for 308, consistent with the claimed 43.5 s per call. scripts/pass_map.py L95-108 `_segment()` already documents and implements the fix ('NOT `ast.get_source_segment`: that re-splits the WHOLE source on every call'). Known-open in the sense that the lesson exists (feedback_method_traps / pass_map.py) but was never applied to this test; finding adds the unfixed site, so keep. Risk 'none' is honest — test-only change, no converter code touched.

**Fix.** Split `src` into lines once and slice by lineno/end_lineno/col_offset (import `scripts.pass_map._segment` as tests/test_pass_map.py L36 already imports the module); assert equality with the old dict once in a probe.

**Proof it landed.** `pytest tests/test_convert_path_parity.py --durations=3` before/after; one-off equality of the two `bodies` dicts over all 308 names.

**Prior art.** scripts/pass_map.py L95-108 `_segment()` + feedback_method_traps entry (78 s -> 0.25 s) — same idiom, fix never propagated to tests/test_convert_path_parity.py

### F060 [MEDIUM] Build-time PYTHONHASHSEED/SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH unset in build_exe.ps1 -- the spec pins only the runtime seed, so same-source builds differ
`scripts/build_exe.ps1:65` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens packaging-deploy · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Two builds of an identical tree produce different bytes, so (a) 'exe CRC' identifies an artefact but never a commit, (b) a 'verify dist matches source' check by hash is impossible, and (c) every `Bin 880490 -> 880490 bytes` base_library.zip churn in the dist commits is noise. This is the same hash-seed mechanism cd63050 fixed for CONVERSION output, left unfixed for the build itself.

**Evidence.** build_exe.ps1:65 `& python -m PyInstaller --noconfirm --clean $spec` with no environment set; the build shell has `PYTHONHASHSEED=[] SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=[]`. CBBEtoUBE.spec:119 `[("hash_seed=1", None, "OPTION")]` — its own comment (lines 106-113) says this pins the FROZEN process's seed, i.e. runtime only. Probe over consecutive dist commits: `_collections_abc.pyc` inside base_library.zip differs 87d14e3->0586500 (both 2026-08-25, same interpreter 3.10.6) and again 14f664b->a871e94: 20 of 32,733 bytes differ, same size, decoded code objects semantically identical (co_code/consts/names all equal), and the differing bytes are two interned names swapping position at offset 13237 — set-iteration order at compile time. Zero date_time differences, so it is not a timestamp.

**Verifier.** Evidence holds: build_exe.ps1:65 `& python -m PyInstaller --noconfirm --clean $spec` sets no env; the spec's `[("hash_seed=1", None, "OPTION")]` (spec:119) is the frozen-process runtime seed per its own comment. No `git log -S PYTHONHASHSEED -- scripts/build_exe.ps1` hit; memory (project_converter_nondeterministic_on_vfs_mods.md:15, feedback_method_traps.md:324) covers only the runtime/source-run seed. The marshal-level pyc diff I did not re-run (triage) -> confidence medium on the mechanism, high on the gap. Risk class: rebuilding with a build-time seed does re-freeze the bundle; the runtime hash_seed OPTION is unchanged so output should be identical, but per rule 5 the finding's own proof already demands a convert-based vertex compare -- so risk is 'vert-neutral' (must be proven), not 'none'.

**Fix.** Set $env:PYTHONHASHSEED='1' and $env:SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=<HEAD commit time> in build_exe.ps1 before the PyInstaller call; document in README 'Building the exe'.

**Proof it landed.** Two -Clean builds with the env set -> identical base_library.zip hash; then rule-5 vertex compare of new exe vs current deployed exe via exe_parity_convert.py before deploying.

**Prior art.** Runtime seed: cd63050 / project_converter_nondeterministic_on_vfs_mods.md. Build-time seed: no prior attempt found.

### F074 [MEDIUM] minimum_push computes an exact dense point-to-rim-edge distance for every region vert when it only consumes a 2.0u threshold — an exact pruned form is ~100x cheaper
`src/fit_metrics.py:417` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens performance · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-open, fix=sound, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Cost is O(region x rim edges): ~5k region verts (push_region_mask, z 86-102, front/side/underside) x every rim edge of the garment (thousands on a dense garment; the profiled 98k-triangle piece). Scratch bench at 5000x3000: dense 1133 ms vs 11 ms for an exact prune (ball query on edge midpoints with radius R + max half-length is a superset of every edge whose closest point is within R; the dense test then runs only on candidate pairs); at 5000x8000: 2983 ms vs 23 ms; verdict mask identical in every trial. This runs once per shape that reaches the push region on the body-swap path (26% of files, and the expensive ones). OPTIMIZATION_LOG already halved it once by restricting to the region (44.0 s -> 6.1 s on a 176 s file, then 8.1% of a 70.2 s file); the 2026-08-23 profile has minimum_push at 6.2% of a 40.8 s conversion, of which this is an unmeasured share. Expected 2-5% of a body-swap conversion; not a lever on the copy path.

**Evidence.** L412-417 `_rim_distance`: `    for s in range(0, len(bV), chunk):` / `        p = bV[s:s + chunk]` / `        ap = p[:, None, :] - a[None, :, :]` / ... / `        out[s:s + chunk] = np.linalg.norm(p[:, None, :] - q, axis=2).min(1)` — a full (chunk x rim_edges x 3) outer difference, twice, for every region vert. Sole consumer L639-641: `    rim_d = _rim_distance(bV[_r], gV, _rim_edges(gT))` / `    reach, _nn = cKDTree(gV).query(bV[_r], k=1)` / `    region[_r] &= (rim_d > PUSH_RIM_MARGIN) & (reach <= PUSH_MAX_REACH)` — only `rim_d > 2.0` is read; the distance itself is never used. Repo-wide grep: `_rim_distance(` has exactly one caller (this line). cKDTree is already imported and used two lines later on the same body verts.

**Verifier.** Confirmed: src/fit_metrics.py L405-419 `_rim_distance` forms a full (chunk x rim_edges x 3) outer difference twice per chunk and returns exact distances; the sole caller L639-641 only thresholds `rim_d > PUSH_RIM_MARGIN` (rg: exactly one call site, L639). A KD prune on edge midpoints with radius R + max half-length is a superset bound, so the thresholded mask is exact by construction. Prior art: OPTIMIZATION_LOG 07-30 (commit ed0093c) already restricted it to the push region (44.0 s -> 6.1 s; 8.1% row at L181); memory project_converter_speed.md L117 lists `_rim_distance` in a ~4.9 s combined bucket and the 08-23 profile has minimum_push at 6.2%. Pruning itself is not proposed or tried anywhere. The 100x bench number is the reader's scratch measurement (not re-run here); share-of-wall claim (2-5% of a body-swap conversion) is an estimate, so confidence medium. Risk 'vert-neutral' is honest ONLY if the mask is identical, which the proof (randomised mask-equality test + convert-based vertex compare, PYTHONHASHSEED=1) correctly requires; the 08-23 profile suggests the whole pass is 6.2%, so the ceiling is small.

**Fix.** Add `_rim_within(bV, gV, rim_e, R)` beside `_rim_distance`: build mid = (a+b)/2, half = |ab|/2; `cKDTree(bV).sparse_distance_matrix(cKDTree(mid), R + half.max(), output_type='ndarray')`; run the existing clamped point-segment formula only on the returned (i, j) pairs; return the boolean `min_d <= R`. Use it at L641 as `~within`. Keep `_rim_distance` for tests/scripts that want the number. Do not change PUSH_RIM_MARGIN semantics (still env-overridable at L457).

**Proof it landed.** As stated; add: the 08-23 profile caps the whole of minimum_push at 6.2% of wall, so report the pass-level before/after share, not just the function's tottime.

**Prior art.** OPTIMIZATION_LOG 07-30 / commit ed0093c restricted `_rim_distance` to the push region; memory project_converter_speed.md L117 flags `_cast`/`_pairs`/`_rim_distance` ~4.9 s combined. Candidate pruning not previously proposed.

### F075 [MEDIUM] The full-vector limb match (12.1% of a body-swap conversion, one call) rebuilds the BODY ray-tester per garment shape and the body weight index per call — three exact hoists inside a pass whose cost has never been split
`src/nif_convert.py:13614` · effort M · risk vert-neutral · lens performance · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-open, fix=sound, confidence=medium

**Claim.** The 2026-08-23 profile (memory) has `_match_limb_motion_to_body` at 13.5% over 8 calls, and `_match_full_weights_to_body` — the ONE instance with `pair_by_ray=True` (L13250) — at 12.1% on its own; the other seven calls total ~1.4%. So the ray-paired instance is an order of magnitude dearer than a family instance, and its cost is attributed in memory to 'ray pairing' without a split between (a) the per-shape body index rebuild (`set_garment`+`tiers` on ~58k triangles per garment shape — sizing: a 29k-point cKDTree builds in 5 ms here, the triangle-centroid tree and 3-4 tier trees plus the gathers are several times that, per shape), (b) the actual cast (`cast_chunked` -> `_pairs` sparse_distance_matrix + `_cast`, inherent, per shape because origins differ), and (c) the python dict/loop work over ~120k body weight pairs per call and every garment pair per shape. All three hoists are exact (same inputs -> same tester, same body_pv, same tree). Which one matters is unknown, which is why this is a measurement-first finding; the per-call body prep also repeats on the four family calls.

**Evidence.** L13446 `    for s in nf.shapes:` encloses L13614-13615: `                        _tester = fit_metrics._ClipTester(` / `                            Vb, body_tris, tmax=float(max_dist) + 2.0)` — Vb and body_tris are per-CALL constants set at L13383-13386 (`bsv = np.asarray(base_shape.verts, ...)`, `Vb = _verts_skin_to_world(...)`, `body_tris = np.asarray(base_shape.tris, ...)`). fit_metrics L211-219 `set_garment` gathers every body triangle, computes centroid+radius and `self.ctree = cKDTree(self.cent)`, and L231-243 `tiers()` builds up to 4 more trees — so each garment shape pays a fresh index over ~58k body triangles. Per call (L13390-13396): `            body_pv = [dict() for _ in range(nb)]` / `            for b, pairs in (base_shape.bone_weights or {}).items():` / `                for vi, w in pairs:` ... / `            tree = _KD(Vb)` — a python loop over every body weight pair plus a 29k-vert tree, and the function is entered from five wrappers per NIF (L13151, L13173, L13196, L13220, L13245), four default ON: L10559 `MATCH_LEG_MOTION = (` / `    not _flag("CBBE2UBE_NO_LEG_MOTION_MATCH", False))`, same shape at L10595 (arm), L10636 (spine), L10767 `MATCH_FULL_WEIGHTS = (` / `    not _flag("CBBE2UBE_NO_FULL_WEIGHT_MATCH", False))`; only L10694 `MATCH_SPINE_TWIST = (` / `    _flag("CBBE2UBE_SPINE_TWIST_MATCH", False))` is OFF. Per shape L13636-13641 builds G by a python loop over every pair: `            for j, b in enumerate(shape_bones):` / `                for vi, w in bw[b]:` / `                    G[iv, j] += float(w)`.

**Verifier.** Confirmed: L13383-13396 set Vb/body_tris/body_pv/tree once per CALL (python double loop over every body weight pair, then `_KD(Vb)`), and L13614-13615 construct `fit_metrics._ClipTester(Vb, body_tris, tmax=float(max_dist) + 2.0)` INSIDE the `for s in nf.shapes:` loop at L13446 — fit_metrics L211-218 `set_garment` gathers all body tris, computes centroid/radius, builds `cKDTree(self.cent)`, and L231-243 `tiers()` builds one tree per radius bucket; all inputs are per-call constants, so the tester is rebuilt identically per garment shape. Flags resolve as claimed (L10559, L10767 default ON via `not _flag(..., False)`; L10694 OFF); L13250 is the sole `pair_by_ray=True` wrapper. Memory project_converter_speed.md 08-23 profile: 13.5% `_match_limb_motion_to_body` (8 calls) / 12.1% `_match_full_weights_to_body`, attributed to 'ray pairing' with no split. The reader correctly makes this measurement-first and correctly defers the np.add.at rewrite (float accumulation order -> chain amplifies noise). Hoists (1)/(2) are exact same-input rebuilds; risk 'vert-neutral' is honest for weights only if the digest-of-bone_weights compare in proof is run in one process. Confidence medium: the size of each of the three components is unmeasured, as the finding itself says.

**Fix.** (1) Build `_tester = fit_metrics._ClipTester(Vb, body_tris, tmax=...)` once per call, above the `for s in nf.shapes:` loop, lazily on first use (tmax is a call constant). (2) Cache (Vb, body_pv, body_tris, tree) per NIF across the five wrapper calls, keyed on a digest of the injected BaseShape's verts+bone_weights (the project already uses digest cache keys, per the nondeterminism memory) rather than object identity, because each wrapper re-opens the NIF. (3) Only after a profile shows (c) matters: build G with `np.add.at(G, (idx, j), w)` from the pairs arrays — NOT before, since accumulation order changes float sums and the chain amplifies float noise. Then cProfile again and keep whichever of the three moved the number.

**Proof it landed.** As stated. Emphasise: hoist (2) (cross-wrapper cache keyed on a digest) must include a HIT assertion, since each wrapper re-opens the NIF and re-injects the BaseShape; a miss on every call is byte-identical and useless (same trap as F115).

**Prior art.** memory project_converter_speed.md 2026-08-23 profile (13.5%/12.1%, 'cost is ray pairing') and its settled note that the five wrapper NIF opens are ~0.25% — do not merge the opens. Per-shape tester rebuild and per-call body_pv/tree rebuild not recorded anywhere.

`src/auto_convert.py:930` · effort M · risk none · lens settings-plumbing · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=sound, confidence=high

A promoted default and a lost flag are indistinguishable in both channels. No memory/docs record an effective-config dump being tried; memory project_current_state.md:156-171 explicitly leans on the `active flags (N)` line as the only run-time evidence, which is exactly the proxy this finding names. Risk 'none' is honest: report/log only.

**Fix.** (1) Add an optional `const: str | None` field to `Setting` naming the module attribute the env var resolves into (most are nif_convert constants), and a ratchet test that for every Setting with a const, a clean-env import gives `getattr(nc, const) == s.default` (polarity-aware) -- generalising test_promoted_defaults' hand table. (2) At run start, after the env echo, resolve every registry row's effective value (`getattr` where const is known, else envflags.flag/knob with s.default) and print a compact "effective settings: N non-default: k=v, ..." line; call the echo from `convert` as well. **Prior art.** memory feedback_method_traps (the proxy-check trap) and feedback_deployed_build_runs_at_defaults describe the symptom; IN_GAME_BUGS.md:3511-3516 asks for a success counter reaching conversion_report.json for one pass; no record of an effective-config dump being designed or tried.

### F077 [MEDIUM] `_known_settings` records keys, not defaults: a default promotion on an existing key is invisible to the unseen-settings NOTE, and a removed key's saved choice is silently discarded
`src/gui_settings.py:1510` · effort S · risk none · lens settings-plumbing · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-open, fix=sound, confidence=high

**Claim.** The NOTE exists to NAME a silent change instead of leaving it inferable from an absent name (the 2026-07-27 lesson). It can only name a key the file has never seen. A user who evaluated an option and left it OFF while OFF was the default stored nothing, and after promotion runs it ON with no line in the log; a user whose json carried an explicit non-default value for a since-removed key loses it with no line either. Both are exactly the "looks like a deliberate choice" ambiguity the field was added to remove.

**Evidence.** unseen_settings: `return True, [s for s in SETTINGS if s.key not in seen and s.env]` (1510); save_values: `out[KNOWN_KEYS_FIELD] = sorted(reg)` (1522) -- key names only; load_values: `s = reg.get(k)` / `if s is not None:` (1468-1469) drops unknown keys without a message, and the next save_values (1518-1519) writes only registered keys, so the choice is gone from disk. env_string_for (1385-1388): "AT THE DEFAULT -> emit NOTHING, so the code's own default applies and a future default change actually reaches the user." git history of the registry: six keys have been removed (arm_twist_pushdown, arm_twist_strength, body_skypatcher, chain_body_shift, full_skypatcher, unified_offset) and seven existing keys had their default flipped (four on 2026-08-22, three on 2026-08-26 per docs/PIPELINE.md:436-448).

**Verifier.** Confirmed. unseen_settings returns `[s for s in SETTINGS if s.key not in seen and s.env]` (gui_settings.py:1510) -- key names only; save_values writes `out[KNOWN_KEYS_FIELD] = sorted(reg)` (1522) and only non-default registered keys (1520-1521); load_values silently drops unknown keys (1468-1470). env_string_for's bool branch emits nothing at the default (1385-1390), so a user who left a key at the old default has no record on disk and a promotion runs it silently. Memory project_current_state.md:538-545 already declares 'THE unseen_settings BUILD SIGNAL IS DEAD' for the saved-file case; this finding adds the specific mechanism (defaults not stored, removed keys dropped) and a fix, which the memory does not. Risk 'none' honest: report only, load stays tolerant.

**Fix.** Store `_known_defaults: {key: default}` beside `_known_settings` in save_values. In unseen_settings (and _warn_unseen_settings) report three lists: keys never seen (today's), keys whose default changed since the save ("X now defaults ON; you had not chosen it"), and keys present in the file that this build no longer has ("your saved Y no longer exists"). Keep load_values tolerant; only the report changes.

**Proof it landed.** Tests/log only. Unit tests in the style of tests/test_unseen_settings.py on a tmp json: (a) a file saved with default False for a key now defaulting True is named; (b) a file carrying a removed key with a non-default value is named; (c) round-trip on the current build reports nothing. No mesh is touched, so no convert comparison applies.

**Prior art.** memory project_current_state.md:538-545 records that the unseen_settings NOTE no longer fires once settings are saved; the missing default/removed-key tracking is the unrecorded cause.

### F086 [MEDIUM] GUI spy tests relaunch an identical child interpreter 6 times; cache the plain spy per module
`tests/test_gui_smoke.py:72` · effort S · risk none · lens test-quality · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Six tests pay ~4 s each to observe the same immutable launch; together with the parity pair, four files account for ~143 s of the 166 s suite, so the 2,300 fast tests are hidden behind two fixable hot spots. The child-process design is justified (the docstring explains the 35 s preflight against the real mod tree), but nothing requires re-launching for every assertion on the same output.

**Evidence.** tests/test_gui_smoke.py:49 `def _launch_in_child(tmp_path, extra_setup="", timeout=120):` ... :72 `        rc = launch_gui(argv=[], auto_close_ms=600, _smoke_settings=True)` ... :81 `    return subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-c", code], capture_output=True,` -- each call is a new Python process that imports src.gui (numpy+scipy+pynifly+nif_convert: 0.51-0.72 s cumulative by importtime), builds the Tk window and waits 600 ms. tests/test_gui_reporting_surface.py:117-129 `_spy(tmp_path)` calls it with the same `extra_setup=_SPY` and parses the same four SPY_* lines; 6 tests call the plain `_spy(tmp_path)` and 5 call `_spy_labels_with_report(tmp_path, report)`; test_gui_armor_tab.py:160 does one more. Measured (`--durations=0` on the GUI/VFS files, 88 passed in 76.24 s): test_gui_reporting_surface.py 47.2 s / 11 tests, test_gui_armor_tab.py 12.0 s / 5, test_gui_smoke.py 2.2 s / 2; the full-suite top-25 lists 14 of these tests at 1.5-4.0 s each.

**Verifier.** Structure confirmed: `_launch_in_child` (tests/test_gui_smoke.py:49-81) spawns `sys.executable -c` per call with `auto_close_ms=600`; `_spy` (test_gui_reporting_surface.py:117-129) re-launches with the identical `_SPY` setup and parses the same four SPY_* lines; 6 call sites use plain `_spy(tmp_path)`. Correction: `_spy_labels_with_report` has 4 call sites (lines 316/333/362/380), not 5 -- the fifth grep hit is the def at :285. Timings not re-run (triage); the ~4 s/launch figure is plausible from the import chain but unverified here. Reframe on the improvement: a module-scoped fixture for the 6 identical spies is the safe win; do NOT chase `auto_close_ms` below 600 -- a Tk window that closes before its idle callbacks run makes `test_a_broken_renderer_actually_fails_this_test` flaky rather than faster, and that test is the control for the whole mechanism. Risk 'none' is honest: tests only, no converter code touched.

**Fix.** Module-scoped fixture (tmp_path_factory) returning the four SPY lists for the 6 plain `_spy(tmp_path)` consumers; keep the 4 per-report launches and the 3 smoke/armor launches. Leave `auto_close_ms=600` alone unless the broken-renderer control is shown to still fail at the lower value across several runs.

**Proof it landed.** `python -m pytest -q --durations=0 tests/test_gui_reporting_surface.py tests/test_gui_smoke.py tests/test_gui_armor_tab.py` before/after; all tests pass; break the renderer in a scratch copy and confirm the control and the 'spy produced no SPY_TABS' assertion still fire.

**Prior art.** none found

### F045 [LOW] Three tracked A/B harnesses and the source-mode GUI/pool child spawn unpinned interpreters; their BYTE diffs carry seed noise and inflate_census still blames 'the writer'
`scripts/analysis/coherence_repair_ab.py:121` · effort S · risk none · lens io-encoding-determinism · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=known-open, fix=reframe, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Rule 5 and the bug-log header say a source-run comparison spanning two invocations is void without PYTHONHASHSEED=1 (three invocations at identical code gave three sha256). These three harnesses do exactly that per arm, so any arm-vs-control difference they report includes seed noise unless the operator remembered to export the variable; inflate_census's own comment shows the noise has already been misattributed to a writer defect. A `python cbbe_to_ube_main.py auto` run (and the source-mode GUI child) spawns per-worker random seeds, so 'source run = one seed' holds only for in-process serial converts.

**Evidence.** coherence_repair_ab.py:119-123: `env = dict(os.environ)` / `env.update(env_extra)` / `r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "scripts/convert_one_armor.py", ...], cwd=str(_REPO), env=env, ...)`; morphtri_gate_ab.py:83-87 identical idiom; inflate_census.py:343-349: `cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "src.auto_convert", "convert", ..., "--workers", str(workers)]` / `subprocess.run(cmd, ..., env=env, ...)` (a POOL, so every spawned worker draws its own seed), and its BASE_ENV comment 118-120: `The NIF hashes DO differ either way -- the writer is nondeterministic in non-geometry bytes` - a claim IN_GAME_BUGS:66-70 declares SUPERSEDED. Only survival_sweep.py:141 `e.setdefault("PYTHONHASHSEED", "1")` and golden_output.py:449-487 `_pin_hash_seed()` pin. grep `PYTHONHASHSEED|hash_randomization|hash_seed` over src/*.py + cbbe_to_ube_main.py: 0 code hits (two comments at nif_convert.py:11017, 15946). gui.py:2400-2403 source-mode child `[sys.executable, str(main_py)] + list(argv_run)` with env built at 2486-2489 and no pin; the spec pins only the frozen exe (CBBEtoUBE.spec:119).

**Verifier.** Evidence confirmed at coherence_repair_ab.py:119-123, morphtri_gate_ab.py:83-87, inflate_census.py:343-349 (env = dict(os.environ); subprocess.run([sys.executable, ...]) with no seed), and the stale BASE_ENV comment 118-120 'the writer is nondeterministic in non-geometry bytes' which IN_GAME_BUGS.md:66-70 supersedes. Full grep: only survival_sweep.py:141 setdefault and golden_output.py:466-480 pin; src/ and main have 0 code hits; spec pins the exe only (CBBEtoUBE.spec:119). gui.py:2402-2403/2486-2489 source child unpinned. BUT the severity is overstated: the bug log (IN_GAME_BUGS:81-93) records that the seed moves BYTES (set-order palette/weight write order) while the SHAPE metric was identical under both seeds, and inflate_census already compares VERTEX data with a same-flags control ('moved 0 verts'), so those harness verdicts are not void - only their byte/sha comparisons and the misattributed 'writer' comment are. Pool per-worker seeding is real in source mode but the log says pool runs differ 0 verts/0 tris (53 weight rows residual). Downgrade to low; improvement (re-exec pin in main when not frozen) is fine and exe-neutral.

**Fix.** Pin once in cbbe_to_ube_main.py (not frozen and sys.flags.hash_randomization -> re-exec with PYTHONHASHSEED=1, golden_output.py:449-480 pattern); delete the stale writer comment at inflate_census.py:118-120. Do not claim prior vertex-metric verdicts are void.

**Proof it landed.** Control-vs-control sha256 of one piece through coherence_repair_ab before/after the pin (byte compare valid: same code, serial). Exe untouched, no vertex compare needed.

**Prior art.** IN_GAME_BUGS.md:81-93 and feedback_method_traps.md:324 record the rule; the three harness spawn sites and the stale inflate_census comment are not recorded.

### F062 [LOW] dist/ history dominates the pack (456 MiB loose, un-gc'd) and still trails the deployed build
`CBBEtoUBE.spec:21` · effort M · risk none · lens packaging-deploy · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-accepted, fix=reframe, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Tracking the bundle costs ~1 MiB of pack per build plus 8.5 MiB loose until gc, and by itself does not stop drift: the drift is between the tools dir and the tree, and it recurs because committing dist is a separate manual step. With a non-reproducible build (previous finding) the tracked blob IS the only record of a build, which argues for keeping it — but only if it lands in the same commit as its source and is self-identifying.

**Evidence.** `git count-objects -vH`: size-pack 128.65 MiB, loose 456.61 MiB (2606 objects, mostly un-gc'd exe blobs). verify-pack by tree: dist/CBBEtoUBE.exe 83.6 MiB in-pack (86 blobs), dist/other 28.6 MiB, openblas DLLs 12.5 MiB, source+docs+tests 1.5 MiB. 212 commits touch dist/ across branches; 133 unique exe blobs (965 MB raw). Owner's reason, 87d14e3: "`dist/` is TRACKED on purpose -- this repo is the exe -- but it had fallen two builds behind the tools dir ... Committing the worktree file skips straight to what is live." Today dist is again modified-uncommitted and the deployed exe (Aug 28) is in no commit. Releases with tags already exist (v1.0…v1.3-alpha, `gh release list` shows 5) and `.gitignore:30 CBBEtoUBE-*.zip` shows release zips are produced — yet 1.3 was never tagged/released.

**Verifier.** Numbers spot-checked: `git count-objects -vH` = count 2606, loose 456.61 MiB, size-pack 128.65 MiB; tags v1.0..v1.3-alpha exist; dist exe last committed 0586500 (08-25) and is modified-uncommitted now; HEAD is 7 ahead of origin/testing. Per-tree blob sums (83.6 MiB exe, 133 blobs) not re-counted -> medium. Tracking dist is an explicit owner decision (87d14e3: 'this repo is the exe'), so known-accepted -- but the finding adds two things the decision did not weigh: the loose-object cost (456 MiB un-gc'd) and that dist still lags the deployed build under the policy. Reframe: the 'commit dist in the same commit as source' recommendation conflicts with the owner's VERIFY-IN-GAME-BEFORE-COMMIT rule (feedback_verify_in_game_before_commit.md) -- a build must be deployed and judged before it is committed, so dist naturally lands after a verdict; the actionable parts are `git gc`, releases/tags, and the build stamp linking exe->commit (F060/F061), not co-commit.

**Fix.** Run `git gc`; attach deployed bundle zips to tags/releases; link exe->commit via a build stamp (F060). Do NOT require dist in the same commit as source -- the owner's rule is deploy, get the in-game verdict, THEN commit, so a dist commit legitimately trails.

**Proof it landed.** count-objects loose ~0 after gc; release asset sha256 equals deployed exe.

**Prior art.** 87d14e3 / project_converter_deploy_path.md: dist tracked on purpose. New here: loose-object bloat and continued drift.

### F104 [LOW] README.md's 'How the refit works' describes the closest-point/barycentric correspondence of the unused experimental branch, not the shipped nearest-vertex warp, and calls the conform 'pull-in only' while it pushes out
`README.md:94` · effort S · risk none · lens docs-currency · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=known-open, fix=reframe, confidence=medium

**Claim.** The README is the stated 'reference for commands, flags and internals' (README.md:17); its refit description matches the experimental branch and the DESIGN reskin that 'does not run on most armour' (DESIGN.md:943-948), so a contributor sizing a change from it would model a smooth surface correspondence where the code snaps to vertices — the very smoothness gap PIPELINE §7 measured.

**Evidence.** README.md:94-100: "1. Find the closest point on the CBBE reference body for every armor vertex — a triangle on the CBBE mesh plus barycentric coordinates inside it. 2. Evaluate the same (triangle, barycentric) on the UBE reference body ... 4. Copy bone weights from the nearest UBE reference vertices (a weighted blend across the k nearest neighbours)". That is `correspondence.compute_deformation` (correspondence.py:24-29 docstring 'Project v onto the CBBE mesh — get (tri index, barycentric coords) ... closest point on the UBE mesh'), reached only from the `warp_armor=True` branch at nif_convert.py:7805-7818 which no caller uses. The shipped path: nif_convert.py:2560-2565 "Build the CBBE->UBE deformation field by nearest-neighbor: each CBBE vert's delta is the vector to its nearest UBE body vert" with `SURFACE_WARP_FIELD = _flag('CBBE2UBE_SURFACE_WARP_FIELD', False)` (:2446) and PIPELINE.md:727-729 'the warp field is a STAIRCASE'. README.md:136-138: "Source-standoff conform — a piece that hugged the 3BA body is reeled back ... pull-in only, with a bust-band exception" vs nif_convert.py:3881 `max_push_out: float = 2.5,` and PIPELINE.md:63 "`conform_to_source_standoff` | pulls IN *and* pushes OUT" and the copy-path call at :7049 with `blend=0.0` used purely for push-out. README.md:117-119 lists 'Anti-poke / adaptive clearance' without noting it never runs on the copy path (PIPELINE.md:238 'Phase 1 has NO anti-poke'; PASS_MAP copy table has no `clear_armor_outside_body` row).

**Verifier.** README.md:94-100 does describe the (tri, barycentric) correspondence, which is `correspondence.compute_deformation` reached only via the dead `warp_armor=True` branch (nif_convert.py:7818); the shipped field is nearest-vertex (:2560-2565, SURFACE_WARP_FIELD default False at :2446). That half stands. The 'calls the conform pull-in only' half is overstated: README.md:138 already says 'pull-in only, with a bust-band exception so the nipple can't poke through', i.e. the push-out exception is documented; the sentence is incomplete (the phase-1 bust clearance push-out is a separate flag) rather than wrong. Reframe to the warp description only.

**Fix.** Rewrite README 'How the refit works' from PIPELINE §2a/§3 (nearest-vertex delta + IDW, inflate, conform gated per path, groove smooth, anti-poke on the body-swap path). Drop the 'pull-in only' correction: README.md:138 already states the bust-band push-out exception; at most add that the opt-in phase-1 bust clearance is a separate push-out.

**Proof it landed.** Doc-only; cross-read against PIPELINE §2a/§3 and PASS_MAP after the edit. No convert needed.

**Prior art.** project_warp_field_default_off.md / project_surface_warp_shape_census.md (surface field default OFF); README's warp description not recorded

### F115 [LOW] The UBE body cKDTree is rebuilt ~20 times per garment shape across the fit chain — an id-keyed cache already exists for two other body arrays and would hit here
`src/nif_convert.py:3297` · effort M · risk vert-neutral · lens performance · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=known-open, fix=sound, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Measured here: one cKDTree build over 29k points = 5.0 ms (query of 5k points = 3.2 ms). ~20 builds per shape ~= 0.1 s per shape, ~0.5 s on the profiled 5-shape body-swap piece (~1.3% of 36-41 s), less on small copy-path pieces. Low by share of wall clock, which agrees with the memory's own 'Tier 2D ... gain = build-cost only (queries dominate) -> modest'. The one thing this audit adds to that note is resolving its stated trap: the memory feared a float32->float64 copy would defeat an id() key; both paths hand every pass the SAME float64 object, so the trap does not apply. Also worth knowing: conform builds the SOURCE body tree per shape too (L3951), and `shape_body_offset` is asked twice per shape on the same shape (L28645 checked, L28661 raw for the frame report) — two builds and four queries where one build serves both.

**Evidence.** Per-shape passes that each rebuild a tree over the constant body: warp L3297 `    tree = cKDTree(cbbe_body_verts)` and L3404 `        ube_tree = cKDTree(ube_v)` (called twice per shape: L28470, L28776 on the body-swap path; L6680, L6933 on the copy path); conform L3951 `    sd, si = cKDTree(src_body_verts).query(src_cloth, k=1)` and L3953 `    ube_tree = cKDTree(ube_body_verts)`; inflate L8062 `    tree = cKDTree(body_verts)` (x2 per shape); snap L7949; clear L5214 `    tree = cKDTree(bv)`; `_inflate_cloth_over_bust_butt` L5534; `_smooth_warp_grooves` L2685; `compute_body_blend_skinning` L19003; `_rigidify_within_clearance` L26498 `        tree = _KD(np.asarray(body_v, dtype=np.float64))` (3 call sites inside the shape loop: L28521, L28944, L29162); `shape_body_offset` L5639 `        tree = _KD(bv)` called twice per shape at L28645 and L28661 plus once per shape inside `_rank_body_layers` (L4500) and `_collect_tri_inputs` (L8899); `bake_preset_into_armor` L8206 `    tree = cKDTree(template)`. The existing pattern: L1331-1341 `_authored_src_tree` (`    key = id(sb)` ... `    if hit is not None and hit[0] is sb:`) and L18877-18889 `_cached_cbbe_body_tree`. The arrays are already float64 at source — L1193 `verts = (np.asarray(base.verts, dtype=np.float64)` in `_cached_ube_body_verts` (threaded as `body_verts_for_fit`, L6523) and L28350-28351 `body_verts_for_p2 = np.asarray(` / `ube_base_for_pass1.verts, dtype=np.float64)` (threaded by identity to every phase-2 pass, e.g. L28474, L28780, L28645) — so the in-function `np.asarray(..., float64)` is a no-op and an `id()`-keyed lookup with identity re-verification hits.

**Verifier.** Confirmed at every spot-checked line: L3297 `tree = cKDTree(cbbe_body_verts)`, L3404 `ube_tree = cKDTree(ube_v)`, L3951/L3953 conform (source AND UBE body trees), L8062 inflate, L26498 `_KD(np.asarray(body_v, dtype=np.float64))`, L5639 `_KD(bv)`; the id-keyed cache pattern with identity re-verification exists at L1331-1341 `_authored_src_tree` and L18877-18889 `_cached_cbbe_body_tree`; the body arrays are float64 at source (L1193, L28350-28351) so the memory's float32 trap does not bite. This is exactly memory project_converter_speed.md candidate 1 (L127) and Tier 2D (L160): proposed, 'Gain UNCONFIRMED', never implemented; the finding's genuine addition is resolving the float32/id() trap and noting `shape_body_offset` is asked twice per shape (L28645/L28661). Count of ~20 builds/shape not re-verified (reader's AST probe, 73 constructions); accepted at medium confidence. Reader's own estimate is ~1.3% of wall, i.e. severity low is right and possibly generous; the 08-23 memory (project_converter_speed.md) already concluded the easy win is gone and queries dominate. Risk 'vert-neutral' is honest (cKDTree deterministic on identical input) provided the proof's HIT assertion and convert-based vertex compare are run.

**Fix.** One helper `_body_tree(arr)` in the `_authored_src_tree` style (id-keyed, keeps the array, re-verifies identity, bounded size), used at every site above where the argument is the threaded body array; keep the per-function `np.asarray` lines. Do not touch trees built on garment/shape arrays. Optional: have L28661 reuse the tree from L28645 by passing it through, since `frame_report` (fit_metrics L550) builds a third one.

**Proof it landed.** As stated; step (1) must be a pass/fail gate: if `cKDTree.__init__` tottime removed is under 1% of wall, record that in OPTIMIZATION_LOG and do not ship (the memory's 08-23 conclusion is that the easy win is gone).

**Prior art.** memory project_converter_speed.md candidate 1 (L127) and Tier 2D (L160): 'Per-pass body cKDTree rebuilds ... Gain UNCONFIRMED ... profile first'; never implemented. Finding adds: the float32->float64 asarray trap does not apply because both paths thread the same float64 object (L1193, L28350).

### F116 [LOW] validate_dst_nif runs an S^2 x V z-fight census on output and re-opened source on every convert, for a warning only — AABB-gate the pairs; profile before caching the source map
`src/nif_convert.py:6019` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens performance · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=reframe, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Scales as S^2/2 pairs x V queries per NIF, twice. Sizing from the same bench: k=1 query of 5k points on a 29k tree = 3.2 ms, so a 30k-vert shape against another costs ~20 ms per pair even with the 0.05u upper bound (the tree still descends per query point). A 5-textured-shape piece: 10 pairs x 2 NIFs ~= 0.4 s (~1% of a 40 s convert); a 10-shape piece ~1.8 s. Small but paid on 100% of files, and it exists only to phrase a warning (z-fight report) — it never edits geometry. The exact cheap gate the project already uses for rays applies here: two shapes whose axis-aligned boxes are more than ZFIGHT_THRESHOLD apart cannot contain a vert pair within it (fit_metrics L1126-1156 `garment_reaches` is the same argument).

**Evidence.** L6011-6013: `        trees = {` / `            s.name: cKDTree(np.asarray(s.verts, dtype=np.float64))` / `            for s in textured` — one tree per textured shape; L6016-6020: `        for i, a in enumerate(textured):` / `            a_verts = np.asarray(a.verts, dtype=np.float64)` / `            for b in textured[i + 1:]:` / `                dists, _ = trees[b.name].query(` / `                    a_verts, k=1, distance_upper_bound=ZFIGHT_THRESHOLD)` — every vert of every shape queried against every other shape; L6028 `        dst_overlap = _pairwise_overlap_counts(nf)` then L6032-6033 `                src_nif = _pynifly().NifFile(filepath=str(src_path))` / `                src_overlap = _pairwise_overlap_counts(src_nif)` — repeated on the source NIF. Called on both paths per convert: L7753 and L30310 (`val_warnings = validate_dst_nif(`). PASS_MAP.md L201/L380 lists it unguarded.

**Verifier.** Confirmed: L6007-6023 `_pairwise_overlap_counts` builds one cKDTree per textured shape then queries every vert of shape a against every later shape b (S^2/2 pairs x V queries, distance_upper_bound=ZFIGHT_THRESHOLD); L6028 runs it on the output and L6032-6033 re-opens the source NIF (`_pynifly().NifFile(filepath=str(src_path))`) and runs it again; `validate_dst_nif(` is called from both entry points (L7753, L30310) unguarded. Output is a warnings list only; no geometry written. No prior art found (memory dir, OPTIMIZATION_LOG, CHANGELOG: `_pairwise_overlap_counts` appears only in the initial source commit 756bf53). Two corrections: (a) the 0.4-1.8 s cost is an extrapolation from a 29k-tree bench, not a profile — and with the 0.05u upper bound cKDTree queries terminate early, so the per-pair cost is likely well under the 20 ms cited; severity low stands, could be trivial. (b) risk 'none' is dishonest as a CLASS: the AABB gate is output-identical by construction, but the source-overlap cache keyed on (path, mtime, size) is a behaviour change to a warning path (a stale cache across a VFS re-resolve could change which warnings print), and rule 5 still demands a convert-based vertex compare (which the proof already concedes). Reframe risk to vert-neutral for the cache half; keep 'none' only for the AABB gate. Also note validate_dst_nif's 'reuse the rays' analogy at fit_metrics `garment_reaches` is illustrative, not code reuse.

**Fix.** Per-shape AABB min/max once; skip pair when boxes are separated by more than ZFIGHT_THRESHOLD on any axis (identical counts by construction). Defer the (path, mtime, size)-keyed source cache until the profile shows the reopen matters; it changes when a warning can go stale.

**Proof it landed.** (1) cProfile serial on a multi-shape piece: cumtime of `_pairwise_overlap_counts` and the source `NifFile` open; if under 1% of wall, log it and stop. (2) Assert the returned `val_warnings` list identical on >=20 pieces, both paths. (3) Convert-based VERTEX compare, PYTHONHASHSEED=1, arms in one harness. Ship the AABB gate alone first (output-identical by construction); the source-map cache is a separate change with its own warnings-equality check across `_0`/`_1`.

**Prior art.** No prior art: `_pairwise_overlap_counts` only in initial commit 756bf53; not in OPTIMIZATION_LOG, CHANGELOG, or memory.

### F117 [LOW] Idiom drift around envflags: 81 raw os.environ reads (11 of them registry vars), 4 call-time `_flag` reads among 137 import-time ones, and a json bool coercion where the string "false" means True
`src/gui_settings.py:1445` · effort S · risk vert-neutral · lens settings-plumbing · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=sound, confidence=medium

**Claim.** None of this is reachable from the GUI's own writes (it emits "1"/"0"/unset, and no raw-read registry bool is a default-ON invert=False row, so the raw idiom never has to understand "0"), but each is a trap for a hand-written recipe or json: a hand-edited `"warp_push_shell_cap": "false"` turns the cap ON; the four call-time flags read the environment at CALL time, so a harness that sets env after import (or monkeypatches a module constant that does not exist for them) A/Bs a different thing from the other 133 -- rule 7's 'resolve flag -> constant -> call site' has no constant to resolve for these four.

**Evidence.** _coerce: `if s.kind == "bool": return bool(v)` (1444-1445) -- executed: `gs._coerce(by_key()["conform_to_body"], "false") -> True`, `"off" -> True`; test_load_ignores_unknown_keys_and_coerces covers only `0 -> False`. envflags.py:17 "The ONE way to read a CBBE2UBE_* environment flag or knob"; AST census: 81 `os.environ.get` + 1 `os.environ[]` reads remain (fit_metrics.py 25, nif_convert.py 21 at module level, e.g. `ANTIPOKE_BUST_CLEAR = float(os.environ.get("CBBE2UBE_BUST_CLEAR", "").strip() or "1.0")` 1022-1023), 11 distinct registry vars read raw (BUST_CLEAR, SMP_ANTIPOKE_PUSH, SEAM_WELD_TOL, GLOW_RIDE_MAX, UBE_BODY, DEBUG_FINALIZE, DEBUG_GLOW_CTRL, NO_WEIGHT_PARITY_CHECK, NO_VANILLA_SWEEP, STANDOFF_BAND_AUDIT, TEXCONV); `_flag` evaluated inside a function body at 24436 (`if not _flag("CBBE2UBE_BODY_COLLIDER", False)`), 25666 (NO_SEAM_WELD), 25747 (NO_SEAM_SKIN_MATCH), 27938 (NO_GLOW_RIDE) versus module level for the other 133.

**Verifier.** Confirmed at every cited line: `_coerce` returns `bool(v)` for kind bool (gui_settings.py:1444-1445) and executing `gs._coerce(by_key()['conform_to_body'], 'false')` returns True; `ANTIPOKE_BUST_CLEAR = float(os.environ.get("CBBE2UBE_BUST_CLEAR", "").strip() or "1.0")` at 1021-1022 reads a registry var (row at gui_settings.py:354) raw; the four in-function `_flag(...)` reads sit at 24436/25666/25747/27938; envflags.py:17-40 is the 'ONE way' contract. Registry rows for BUST_CLEAR, SMP_ANTIPOKE_PUSH, NO_VANILLA_SWEEP, SEAM_WELD_TOL, UBE_BODY, TEXCONV exist (354/1096/1178/1196/1207/1210). The 81/137/11 census not re-counted (raw grep: 37 `os.environ` in nif_convert, 25 in fit_metrics -- consistent in magnitude). Memory project_scratchpad_tooling.md:19 and project_comment_audit_2026_08_17.md:33 note the idiom collapse and the bare UBE_BODY read but not the residual set or the _coerce edge. Risk 'vert-neutral' is honest given the proof it demands (convert-based vertex compare); hoisting call-time to import-time reads is neutral only in a clean-env run, which the proof covers.

**Fix.** Route the remaining raw reads through envflags.flag/knob (the registry ones first) and hoist the four call-time `_flag` reads to module constants (BODY_COLLIDER, NO_SEAM_WELD, NO_SEAM_SKIN_MATCH, NO_GLOW_RIDE) so every flag has the same evaluation time; extend the idiom ratchet in test_every_gui_env_is_read_by_src to assert registry vars are read via envflags only; make _coerce reject/normalise strings for bool kind (accept true/false/1/0/yes/no, else default) and add the "false" case to the coercion test.

**Proof it landed.** Hoisting reads is a refactor and is only behaviour-neutral if a convert-based VERTEX comparison says so -- one piece per path, same harness, both arms, PYTHONHASHSEED=1 on source runs, byte compare invalid. Unit tests: _coerce("false") is False; the four constants exist and equal the clean-env default; the ratchet reports 0 registry vars read raw.

**Prior art.** envflags.py's own docstring records the 2026-08-18 collapse (366 reads, twelve variants) and its two sharp edges; the residual raw/ call-time set and the _coerce edge are not recorded.

AST sweep over 2109 test functions: `all(genexp)`/`not any(genexp)` = 38 hits, every one floored (literal population, subscript, sibling positive test, or an explicit control -- test_resort_masters.py:76-84 now carries the 08-22 control `assert any(x.startswith("master-ordering") for x in ube_patcher.validate_patch(_pre, ...))`); `assert True`/`len(x) >= 0`/`count >= 0` = 0; bare `pytest.MonkeyPatch()` = 0; assert-less tests = 5, all must-not-raise calls (test_active_flag_echo.py:84-89 `ac._echo_active_experiment_flags()  # must not raise`). For-loops containing an assert: 230; over a non-literal population with no floor in the same function: 51 (test_gui_settings 6, test_pose_set 5, test_chain_rest_lift 4, ...); no floor on the same names anywhere in the file: 23; after reading each, 3 are over populations owned by src (ps.REGION_POSES, ps.POSE_SET, gs.SETTINGS), the rest iterate test-local literals or are floored via a subscript/`== [...]` my detector did not credit.

**Verifier.** Evidence confirmed at test_pose_set.py:34-36, 68-72, 78-80 and test_gui_settings.py:204-207; no `len(gs.SETTINGS|ps.POSE_SET|ps.REGION_POSES)` floor anywhere in tests/; IN_GAME_BUGS.md:1858 does say 'The vacuous shapes are exactly two'. Scope correction: the three src-sourced instances are NOT actually vacuous under an emptied table -- an empty `POSE_SET` raises KeyError at test_pose_set.py:54 `ps.POSE_SET["bind"] == []`; an empty `REGION_POSES` fails :29-31 as long as `ps.REGIONS` is non-empty; an empty `gs.SETTINGS` fails test_gui_settings.py:320 `gs.by_key()["rigid_majority_softbody"]`. The finding half-admits this ('other tests would notice'). ### F011 [HIGH] conform (and the groove authored-cap) read a ZERO source standoff at defaults while the same function already recomputes the normals for the chest pass; the fix is built and reachable but a recorded collider regression (461->467) and the owed conform retune block the flip, not a missing census

**Claim.** This is the textbook rule-1 case the DESIGN doc describes ("a stage acting blind"): conform is fed a target that is identically zero on the body-swap path, makes large bogus pull-ins, and `_smooth_warp_grooves` + `#groove-authored-cap` + the anti-poke then spend budget undoing it. The producer fix is built, reachable, in-game confirmed on the reported piece, measured to reduce every downstream repair's workload, and is still OFF because its own comment asks for a ~20%-of-verts census that has not been run in two weeks — while promotions with less evidence (chord, surface guard) went default-ON on 08-26.

So how much cleanup is still needed is a function of how well the passes upstream behave". memory/project_conform_target_was_zero.md: "CENSUS: 186 of 186 source pieces with a NAMED inline body have all-zero normals -- 100%"; "CONFIRMED IN GAME 2026-08-16: 'pauldrons work.'"; "`_smooth_warp_grooves` sits immediately after it, cleaning up after it". memory/project_pass_damage_ledger.md (34 body-swap shapes): s04_conform creates penetration 476 -> 46, creates stretch 1831 -> 540 with SRC_NORMAL_FIX=1; downstream panel_rigidity creates 4721 -> 4055, antipoke repairs 4902 -> 3894; median stretched-edge rate per shape 0.005% -> 0.000%. GUI row exists (`src_normal_fix`).

**Verifier.** Core claim confirmed: at defaults `src_body_n_p2` is the STORED (all-zero) normals (28288 `_body_normals_or_compute(...) if _SRC_NORMAL_FIX else getattr(cbbe_body_shape, "normals", None)`), and it feeds `conform_to_source_standoff` at 28871, so on every named inline body with zero normals the conform target is identically 0; the fix is reachable (gui_settings.py:456-458 Setting row, default False) and the ledger/in-game records the finding cites are accurate. CORRECTIONS that change the scope and the improvement: (1) The zeroed array reaches MORE than conform at defaults -- it is also passed to `_smooth_warp_grooves` for `#groove-authored-cap` (28901) -- and to three opt-ins (authored-inflate 28809, authored-antipoke 29130, rebury 29235); flipping the flag changes all of them, not one pass. (2) The same function already recomputes normals UNCONDITIONALLY at 29670 (`src_body_n_p2 = (_body_normals_or_compute(cbbe_body_shape) ...` for the chest/abdomen depth passes, comment 'Source body normals often zeroed in BodySlide output; compute from tris'), so phase 2 is internally inconsistent: real normals for one pass, zero for conform -- a stronger rule-1 argument than the finding makes, and unrecorded anywhere. (3) 'DEFAULT OFF since 08-16' is wrong: it has been default OFF since inception (flag predates 2026-08-12; blame 1700-1704 -> 4968712 08-12); 08-16 is the in-game confirmation date. (4) The in-game confirmation was ONE hand-deployed piece under a three-flag recipe (`PANEL_RIGIDITY=0.75 SRC_NORMAL_FIX=1 PANEL_RIGID_RIDE=1`, memory project_layer_ride_discards_fit:126-127); the stage-dump attribution to conform is direct, but no PACK with the flag on was ever judged (the 08-17 reconvert ran at defaults; every later settings file lacked it). (5) MISSED PRIOR ART -- the reason it is off is not merely an unrun census: memory project_current_state.md 08-24 records 'STILL OFF ON PURPOSE: src_normal_fix (... BREAKS skirt colliders -- 461 -> 467 verts)' and IN_GAME_BUGS.md:702-704 'that is `_SRC_NORMAL_FIX`, a DIFFERENT (real) defect found the same hour' -- an unresolved regression on the GENERATED collider proxy under the flag, with no topic memory and no explanation. (6) The constant's own comment (1700-1704) makes the flip conditional on RETUNING the conform constants with the fix on ('every fit constant here was tuned over dozens of in-game cycles WITH the zeroed normals in play'); the improvement omits the retune.

**Fix.** Fix the producer once: make phase 2 read `_body_normals_or_compute(cbbe_body_shape)` at 28288 unconditionally (it already does at 29670), so conform, groove-authored-cap and the authored opt-ins all see the same real normals -- but gate that flip on three things the finding omits: (a) explain and clear the 461->467 generated-collider delta (SkirtCol/ButtCol vert counts, `registered_bone_audit`, `proxy_encloses_chain` = 0 paired against control); (b) re-measure `#groove-authored-cap` with a true target, since it changes with the same array; (c) retune or at least re-validate the conform constants with the fix on, as the constant's own comment requires. **Verifier.** All cited sites exist: copy path lifts via _shape_global_to_skin/_verts_skin_to_world (L6619, L6627, L6880) and restores at L7410; phase 2 uses shape_body_offset (L28645-28646, removed L29511-29512) and its fine-anim branch uses raw verts (L28431); the docstring census at L5583-5591 is as quoted. Two extra facts strengthen the improvement: a shared helper at L4499-4500 ALREADY applies g2s lift THEN shape_body_offset (the 'one frame' the finding asks for), and the shared tail at L8899 uses offset-only - so today there are three frame conventions, not two. **Fix.** Use the L4499-4500 composition (g2s lift, then geometric-checked offset) as the single shared frame helper for both entry paths, both sub-branches and the L8899 tail site; report frame_report on the copy path too.

Resolution goes through `_read_source_hdt_xml_text(dst_path)` memoised on the dst NIF's (path, mtime_ns, size) (25229-25237) with the source-bound text used ONLY when the dst pointer fails to resolve (25270-25271). src/auto_convert.py:260 `fut_to_item = {self.pool.submit(fn, it): it for it in items}` - one pool task per NIF, so `_0` and `_1` of a pair run concurrently on different workers.

Every cited line does what is claimed: `_finalize_hdt_physics` strips the weight suffix (24668-24674 `for suf in ("_0", "_1"): ... dst_xml_disk = dst_path.parent / f"{stem}.xml"`) and `atomic_copy(str(src_xml), str(dst_xml_disk))` (24713) overwrites the shared file with the pristine authored copy, then `_ensure_cloth_body_collider`/`_harden_hdt_xml_for_fsmp`/`_make_chains_static` (24870/24876/24882) read+rewrite it; `_split_bust_collider_xml` (17494-17501) REPOINTS `<per-triangle-shape name="X">` to `XCol` in that same file and writes it back with `atomic_write_bytes(xml, ...)`; `_add_butt_collider_patch` (16955) and `_add_skirt_collider_proxy` (17425) append blocks to it; the sequence 8624-8658 runs all of this then `_transfer_body_jiggle_to_fitted`, which has no weight gate (15660-15663 both weights run) and skips on `s.name in collider_names` (15678+45). The readers resolve through `_read_source_hdt_xml_text(dst_path)`: memo key is the DST nif's (path, mtime_ns, size) (25229-25233), primary route is `_read_source_hdt_xml_disk(dst)` -> the dst nif's extra-data pointer -> `_resolve_data_rel_in_vfs` local root = the OUTPUT tree (24148-24155) -> `xml_disk.read_text` (25284); the source-bound `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` is consulted ONLY when that fails (25270-25271). `auto_convert.py` builds one work item per NIF (1694-1735, `_0` and `_1` are separate `resolved_pairs` entries) and submits them all to a ProcessPoolExecutor (260, `ProcessPoolExecutor` at 52/210), so the pair really runs concurrently in different processes with disk as the only shared state. Concrete bimodal, weight-only, SMP-only divergence I can name from the code: worker A's bust-split repoint makes `Garment` a non-collider and `GarmentCol` the collider in the shared XML; if worker B's finalize `atomic_copy` (pristine) lands before A's jiggle graft reads, A sees `Garment` back in `collider_names` and SKIPS grafting it -- a weight-row-only change on A's NIF that depends on scheduling; serial is deterministic because `_1` always writes last. Scope corrections: (1) the 'momentarily absent after 23816' window exists ONLY for GENERATED XMLs (23815 gates the unlink on the `Auto-generated by cbbe-to-ube` header); authored copies go through `atomic_copy`/`atomic_write_bytes` = os.replace (atomic_io.py 97-143), so for the authored class the race is old-content-vs-new-content, never absence. (2) dst-path readers of `_hdt_collider_shape_names` are 10 of 16 call sites (8826 audit guard, 11808, 12144, 12695, 13335, 14272, 15678, 16378 shrinkwrap -- default OFF `COLLIDER_SHRINKWRAP=_flag(...,False)` 16350, 20314, 24990 `_reauthor_nif_fresh` preserve-set), not seven; and of the seven named, 12144 `_selfint_overrides` is a VERTEX pass (docstring 12116-12120), so six weight passes + the reauthor preserve gate are the weight-reaching readers. (3) Whether this IS the 53-row residual is unproven -- the finding correctly calls it a candidate; nothing here elevates it beyond that. Prior art: IN_GAME_BUGS ~965-975 fix part (b) 'Do not depend on the destination file ... carry it through the passes' was RECOMMENDED for BUG-00, but the shipped 3b714ed (2026-08-19) built only the fallback (25270), so (b) is recorded-but-unbuilt; the memory names 'a shared-file timing interaction between the pair' for the TRI only. The XML as the second shared sidecar, the partner-worker (not same-run) write ordering, and the repoint/append/pristine-overwrite interleavings are new. Rules: fix (a) single-writer / source-bound reads is at the producer (rule 1) and class-wide (rule 2); risk=behaviour is honest (SMP-piece weights change on one draw). Reframe of the improvement: (a) must ALSO cover the passes' need to see this worker's OWN repoint/append (bust-split names, ButtCol, SkirtCol) -- derive from `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` plus an in-process overlay of the decls this worker rewrote, or the split invariant (17491 'garment AND clone both present') silently flips; and the shipped XML itself must be single-writer (owning variant via `_tri_is_owning_variant` 8250) or the last finalize's `_harden(..., nf)` prunes against whichever NIF finished last.

**Fix.** Producer fix in two parts: (a) make `_hdt_collider_shape_names`/`_hdt_softbody_shape_names` answer from `_PIECE_HDT_XML_TEXT` (bound at 6384) plus an in-process overlay of the decls THIS worker rewrote (bust-split repoints, ButtCol, SkirtCol), never from the destination file; delete the dst route (25246-25284 primary) so the race cannot return. (b) make the shared `<stem>.xml` single-writer per piece, the way `#tri-write-once` does it (`_tri_is_owning_variant`, 8250), or have each worker write `<stem>_<w>.xml.part` and merge once after the pool; note the last-writer's `_harden_hdt_xml_for_fsmp(..., nf)` currently prunes against whichever NIF finished last. Generated XMLs (23816 unlink) need the same owner rule.

**Proof it landed.** Mechanism first, in ONE process (PYTHONHASHSEED=1): convert the `_1` of one of the two named SMP residual pieces with a spy on `_hdt_collider_shape_names`, once with the shared <stem>.xml held at the pristine copy and once after this piece's own bust-split/butt-patch state, and assert the `w_content` rows of `_transfer_body_jiggle_to_fitted`'s targets differ -- that proves a dst read reaches weights. Acceptance: two 16-worker DEPLOYED-exe runs on the 84-NIF VFS harness -> `w_content` 53 -> 0 with `verts`/`tris` still 0; `--workers=1` before/after weight-identical for every non-SMP piece (convert-based vertex+weight compare, not bytes); then the collapse case from IN_GAME_BUGS ~985 and an in-game look at an SMP piece before commit. Run `verify_zero_weight_bones.py` after, as with any weight change.

**Prior art.** IN_GAME_BUGS.md ~965-975 (BUG-00 fix part (b) 'Do not depend on the destination file ... carry it through the passes' -- recommended, NOT built; 3b714ed shipped the fallback at nif_convert.py:25270 only) and memory project_converter_nondeterministic_on_vfs_mods.md 'Untested leading suspect: a shared-file timing interaction between the pair' (TRI only). New here: the XML is the shared file, the partner-worker interleavings (bust-split repoint 17494-17501 / ButtCol+SkirtCol appends / pristine `atomic_copy` 24713 / generated-only unlink 23816), and the dst-reader census.

### F012 [HIGH] NIF work is dispatched per mod behind a blocking barrier; the cross-mod queue planned in OPTIMIZATION_LOG (a ~20% CEILING on a stale 07-30 baseline) is unimplemented
`src/auto_convert.py:1797` · effort L · risk behaviour · lens performance · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=known-open, fix=reframe, confidence=medium

**Claim.** Parallelism is capped by each mod's file count: a six-file mod occupies six of sixteen workers and the other ten idle until it finishes, then the next mod starts. The project's own full-pack measurement (103.8 min NIF phase, 3,653 files) put 42.3 min of wall clock in under-filled batches and ~21 min (20%) as recoverable by one cross-mod queue; the ten most expensive batches are 26% of all worker-seconds AND under-filled, so the slow mods pay twice. Nothing has changed in the dispatch structure since that measurement: the shared _NifPool persists across mods (settled) but every mod still waits for its own last file before the next mod's items are submitted. By expected share of wall clock this is the largest item in this lens and the only one the project has already measured end-to-end.

**Evidence.** L1797: `                nif_pool.run_batch(work_items, _on_result)` — called inside auto_convert_mod, once per source mod. L267-268 in _NifPool._run_parallel: `        for fut in as_completed(fut_to_item):` / `            it = fut_to_item[fut]` — returns only when every item of THIS mod has completed. L3034 in convert(): `        for i, src in enumerate(sources, 1):` — sources are processed strictly sequentially, with ESP generation for the mod inside the same auto_convert_mod call. Grep for cross-mod / one-queue in src/ and docs/ finds only OPTIMIZATION_LOG.md L38, L83, L109 (the plan), nothing in code. docs/worklog/OPTIMIZATION_LOG.md L80-84: `| batches using fewer than 16 workers | **92 of 131** |` / `| recoverable by one cross-mod queue | **~21 min (20%)** |`.

**Verifier.** Structure CONFIRMED: `nif_pool.run_batch(work_items, _on_result)` (src/auto_convert.py L1797) is called once per mod inside auto_convert_mod, `_run_parallel` drains `for fut in as_completed(fut_to_item)` (L267) before returning, and `for i, src in enumerate(sources, 1)` (L3034) walks sources strictly serially; `rg -c -i 'cross-mod|per-mod barrier'` over src/docs/tests/CHANGELOG/IN_GAME_BUGS gives auto_convert.py:2 (both comments about the pool's no-cascade contract, L196/L1298), OPTIMIZATION_LOG.md:2, one test comment -- no queue exists in code, and `git log -S'cross-mod' -- src` shows no attempt was ever made. The NUMBERS are only partly checkable: 22.1 worker-h / 16 = 82.9 min ideal -> 20.9 min = 20.2% (my recount) matches the log's '~21 min (20%)', but that figure is an arithmetic CEILING (perfect packing) not a measured recovery, and '92 of 131', '42.3 min', '26%' cannot be recounted from the repo -- the full-pack run log is gitignored (`.gitignore:17 *.log`; the only log present, CBBEtoUBE_last_run.log, is a single 46-file mod from 08-07). The baseline is v1.2.2 (07-30) and predates 3c4d73d (copy-path seam weld 160.1s->8.78s on 78% of files), the standoff-audit default-off (17.3% of a body-swap), and the morph 2.4x -- the ten most expensive under-filled batches were exactly the slow copy-heavy mods those fixes shrank, so the absolute 21 min is STALE and the share is unknown until re-measured. Two errors in the improvement: (1) ESP generation is NOT a post stage -- `# --- ESPs ---` is L1392, BEFORE `# --- NIFs ---` L1659, and `converted_rel_paths` (L1541-1553) is derived from `resolved_pairs`, never from written NIFs, so ESP gen stays in PLAN; the real POST is the load-check + VirtualBody hide (L1851-1870, re-reads `planned_output_nifs` from disk), textures (independent) and the report (L1905). (2) The GUI's ETA is an EWMA of the gap BETWEEN `[progress]` markers (gui.py L173-192, parsed at L2669) -- a queue that plans every mod up front emits all markers at once and breaks the determinate bar, so the marker must move to per-mod POST completion. Proof correction: the per-mod `NIF conversion: N files in Xs` note (L1807-1809) is meaningless under a queue (mods overlap), so arm B must be timed on total elapsed + per-mod completion timestamps, not per-mod elapsed. Risk 'behaviour' is honest: items are self-contained tuples (L1729-1734) and `_nif_convert_worker` (L71-93) holds no per-item state, so vertices SHOULD be identical, but scheduling order and worker-cache warm order change and the pool-only 53-weight-row residual already exists -- not vert-neutral by claim.

**Fix.** Split auto_convert_mod at L1659: PLAN = everything through work_items (source/ESP resolution incl. the ESP stage L1392-1657, which is upstream of the plan and never reads written NIFs, dst claims via claimed_dst_paths, incremental skip); POST = textures, the load-check + VirtualBody hide (L1851-1870), notes/report. Plan mods in load order so first-writer-wins claims are byte-for-byte today's; submit each mod's items to the one shared _NifPool tagged with the mod owner; run a mod's POST when ITS futures complete; emit the `[progress]` marker at POST completion (not at plan time) so the GUI EWMA ETA (gui.py L173-192) keeps working. _NifPool crash recovery must re-run `remaining` per owner so only that mod's POST waits. Vanilla sweep stays last with its serial retry.

**Proof it landed.** (1) Re-measure FIRST on current code (the 07-30 figures predate the copy-path 18x and standoff-off wins): from one full-pack run's per-mod `NIF conversion: N files in Xs ... with W worker(s)` notes recompute under-filled wall and the packing ceiling; if the ceiling is under ~10% of the NIF phase the item drops below the per-file work in project_converter_speed.md. (2) Interleaved A/B (>=3 reps per arm, warm pool, quiet machine, `python -u`, `--workers 16`) on the same >=20-mod subset timing TOTAL elapsed and per-mod COMPLETION timestamps -- per-mod elapsed is undefined under a queue. (3) Convert-based VERTEX compare of every output NIF between arms, PYTHONHASHSEED=1, weights compared on weights, within one harness (exe_parity_convert.py style). (4) Assert identical (src,dst,status) ConvertResult rows, identical claimed_dst_paths collision list, identical per-mod ESP outputs and espgen snapshots. (5) Poison-NIF injection: only that item errors, every other mod's items and POST stages complete; GUI marker count equals len(sources).

**Prior art.** docs/worklog/OPTIMIZATION_LOG.md 'Plan, in order' item 2 (queued 07-30, never started; git log -S'cross-mod' -- src is empty of implementation). Memory project_converter_speed.md 2026-07-13 tiered plan 'Tier 3F: confirm worker saturation; pipeline the serial tail to overlap with straggler NIFs' -- same idea, also unimplemented.

### F013 [MEDIUM] Headless `auto` never applies CBBEtoUBE_settings.json although the same run reads it to print settings advice, and README/USING call `auto` the button's pipeline
`src/auto_convert.py:5181` · effort M · risk behaviour · lens settings-plumbing · verifier: real=partly, prior-art=known-open, fix=reframe, confidence=high

**Claim.** The json -> env translation happens only in the GUI process's subprocess launch. A headless `auto` (README's documented path, any script, an MO2 executable entry that passes `auto`) runs at code defaults + whatever CBBE2UBE_* happens to be in the environment, silently discarding every saved toggle -- and the log actively misleads: the flag echo prints "active flags: none (all defaults)" and the unseen-settings NOTE lists only options the file has never seen, implying the ones it HAS seen were applied. This is the exact class that cost the 2026-07-27 reconvert (settings that never reached the run, normal-looking log). It also means the answer to "json vs env, which wins?" depends on the launch path: GUI-child = json authoritative for registry vars (apply_env pops them, gui_settings.py:1420), headless = env only; nothing in the log says which path produced a pack.

**Evidence.** _cmd_auto opens with `_echo_active_experiment_flags()` (5181) then `lay = paths.discover_layout(); paths.export_to_env(lay)` (5182-5183); the only consumers of gui_settings.load_values/apply_env in src/ are gui.py:435, 450, 2486 (`env = gui_settings.apply_env(state.get("settings") or {}, base_env=dict(os.environ))` inside the GUI's child-launch `_worker`); auto_convert.py's single gui_settings import is line 958 inside `_warn_unseen_settings`, which prints (963) "They run at their DEFAULT, which is not the same as you having chosen it" and "Open the GUI's Settings tab and save once to record them." README.md:291-297 advertises `CBBEtoUBE.exe auto` as the "headless one-click pipeline over the whole modlist ... (what the GUI's convert button runs under the hood)" and 392 "Settings persist to `CBBEtoUBE_settings.json` beside the exe"; USING.md:38-41 "The convert button runs the same pipeline as: CBBEtoUBE.exe auto".

**Verifier.** Mechanics CONFIRMED by independent trace: `apply_env` has exactly ONE consumer in src/ (gui.py:2486, inside the GUI's child-launch `_worker`); `main()` (auto_convert.py:5593-5594) dispatches `auto` straight to `_cmd_auto`, whose first statements are `_echo_active_experiment_flags()` / `paths.discover_layout()` / `paths.export_to_env(lay)` (5181-5183) with no settings load anywhere; `cbbe_to_ube_main._run()` (184-187) only does `from src.auto_convert import main; return main()`. The same run DOES read the json via `_warn_unseen_settings` -> `gs.unseen_settings()` -> `config_path()` (auto_convert.py:958-959, gui_settings.py:1494) to print the 'They run at their DEFAULT' NOTE, which implies file-seen options were applied. README.md:296-297 and USING.md:38-41 assert `auto` is 'what the GUI's convert button runs under the hood' -- false for settings. The GUI child really is `['auto', ...]` (gui.py:2349) with the json translated by apply_env, so the two launch paths diverge exactly as claimed. The improvement's 'must be the entry script' point is also right: nif_convert.py holds 214 module-level `NAME = _knob(...)` reads (rg count) and auto_convert.py:56 imports nif_convert at import time. CORRECTIONS: (1) 'the exact class that cost the 2026-07-27 reconvert' overstates it -- every record of 07-27 (test_active_flag_echo.py docstring, PLAN_GUI_ARMOR_TAB.md:102, unseen_settings docstring 1482-1485) is a GUI-path failure (file edited while GUI open; two new options default-OFF), never a headless run; same family, different mechanism. (2) Severity HIGH -> MEDIUM: the deployed user path (MO2 -> no args -> GUI -> child `auto` + apply_env) is unaffected; memory records the frozen windowed exe dying silently when launched headless from a shell (07-04), so the exposed population is source runs / scripts; and no CBBEtoUBE_settings.json exists at the repo root today (only CBBEtoUBE_last_failures.json), so a source `auto` currently has nothing to discard unless CBBE2UBE_CONFIG points at the deployed file. (3) The echo line itself is a documented design choice (docstring 925-927: 'Deliberately prints the raw environment rather than the settings file'); the misleading element is the unseen-settings NOTE + README/USING, not the echo. KNOWN-OPEN: memory project_converter_deploy_path.md:132-133 'CLI does NOT read the settings file -- only the GUI applies them' and project_faithful_validation_harness.md:42-45 record it as a hand-run trap, never as a defect or a reasoned decision; `git log -S load_values`/`-S config_path` over auto_convert.py and cbbe_to_ube_main.py return nothing, so never attempted. The finding adds the doc contradiction, the misleading NOTE and a concrete fix -> keep. REFRAME of the fix: (a) if headless `auto` starts reading the file beside the exe/repo, every deployed-exe or source headless harness silently inherits the user's toggles -- violates the A/B-within-one-harness discipline unless the apply is announced ('settings file: N override(s) applied from <path>') and a `--no-settings` (or opt-in `--settings`) exists; `convert` must stay env-only. (b) The proof section's 'GUI-child path must be vertex-neutral' is NOT automatic: apply_env POPS default-valued registry vars (gui_settings.py:1419-1421), so if the child re-reads the FILE it can re-add a value the GUI's in-memory state had at default (unsaved change, or file edited while GUI open -- the 07-27 mechanism in reverse). The GUI parent must mark the child (e.g. CBBE2UBE_SETTINGS_APPLIED=1) and the child must skip the file when marked; only then is the child path json-neutral and worth the vertex compare. Effort M, risk behaviour (headless converted verts change by design).

**Fix.** Apply the file in cbbe_to_ube_main._run() for the `auto` subcommand BEFORE `from src.auto_convert import main` (nif_convert holds 214 import-time _knob reads and auto_convert.py:56 imports it at module import): build `env = gui_settings.apply_env(load_values(), base_env=dict(os.environ))` then os.environ.update -- but with explicit-env-wins precedence for headless and an announcement line 'settings file: N override(s) applied from <path> / env overrides: M'. Gate it: the GUI parent sets a marker (e.g. CBBE2UBE_SETTINGS_APPLIED=1) so the child never re-reads the file (apply_env pops default-valued vars, so a re-read could resurrect an unsaved/stale file value -- the 07-27 mechanism in reverse). Give harnesses `--no-settings`; keep `convert` env-only and print a one-line notice when a json beside the exe holds overrides that were not applied. Fix README.md:296-297 / USING.md:38 to state the difference. Add the launch path (gui-child / headless) to the echo line.

**Proof it landed.** Headless: no neutrality claim (change is by design). GUI child: with the marker in place the child env is byte-equal to today's; assert that with a unit test (spawn-free: build env via the new helper with the marker set and compare to apply_env output), then one convert-based VERTEX compare of a GUI-launched pack piece in a single harness (deployed exe, PYTHONHASHSEED=1 for any source arm). Unit: tmp json via CBBE2UBE_CONFIG with `panel_rigid_ride=false`, `subprocess.run([sys.executable,'cbbe_to_ube_main.py','auto','--list-only'], env=...)` with CBBE2UBE_MODS_ROOT at a tmp dir -> echo contains PANEL_RIGID_RIDE=0; env pre-set to 1 -> documented precedence; marker set -> file ignored; source-inspection test that the apply precedes the import.

**Prior art.** Recorded as a hand-run trap, not a defect: memory project_converter_deploy_path.md:132-133 'CLI does NOT read the settings file -- only the GUI applies them' (2026-07-04) and project_faithful_validation_harness.md:42 'GUI settings the CLI never reads'. Never attempted (git -S load_values/config_path on the entry files: nothing). The 07-27 reconvert was a GUI-path failure (file edited while GUI open / new options default-OFF), not this path.

### F046 [MEDIUM] Plugin strings are decoded utf-8/ignore and re-encoded utf-8 unconditionally - any non-ASCII (cp1252) byte in a model path or master name is silently dropped and the record rewritten to a path that does not exist
`src/ube_patcher.py:930` · effort S · risk behaviour · lens io-encoding-determinism · verifier: real=confirmed, prior-art=new, fix=sound, confidence=high

**Claim.** Skyrim plugin zstrings are single-byte cp1252; a byte >= 0x80 that is not valid UTF-8 (e.g. an accented letter in a folder name, common in non-English armour mods) is DROPPED by errors="ignore" on read and the record is written back pointing at a mesh/master that is not on disk - an invisible piece or a missing master, with nothing reported. Same root as BUG-12 (implicit/wrong Windows text codec on a round trip), on the plugin side. The class is 'any MOD2-5 or MAST value with a non-ASCII byte'; its frequency in the live load order is unmeasured.

**Evidence.** 930: `path = data.rstrip(b"\x00").decode("utf-8", errors="ignore")`; 932: `new_path = (path_prefix + path) if converted else path`; 959: `out += esp.encode_subrecord(sig, esp.encode_zstring(new_path))` - re-emitted from the DECODED string even when unchanged. src/esp.py:57-59: `def encode_zstring(s: str) -> bytes: """zstring: null-terminated UTF-8.""" return s.encode("utf-8") + b"\x00"`; esp.py:267 `pending_mast = sd.rstrip(b"\x00").decode("utf-8", errors="ignore")` re-emitted at 286 `encode_subrecord(b"MAST", encode_zstring(m))`. The SAME field family is decoded as cp1252 in src/auto_convert.py:2643 `s = dd.rstrip(bytes(1)).decode("cp1252", "replace")` and as latin-1 at ube_patcher.py:349 and 2463 - three codecs for one field type.

**Verifier.** Confirmed at ube_patcher.py:930 `decode("utf-8", errors="ignore")`, 932 `new_path = (path_prefix + path) if converted else path`, 959 re-emit via `esp.encode_zstring(new_path)` even when unchanged; esp.py:57-59 encodes UTF-8; esp.py:267 MAST decoded utf-8/ignore and re-emitted at 286. Three codecs for the same field family confirmed: latin-1 at ube_patcher.py:288,349,785,2414,2463,3203 and cp1252 at auto_convert.py:2643, bsa_strings.py:327. Consequence chain holds: a >=0x80 byte invalid as UTF-8 is dropped, converted_nif_exists(path) then fails on the mangled path, and the record is re-written with the mangled path -> missing mesh/master, silently. Memory grep (cp1252/encode_zstring/MAST): nothing on the plugin side; git log -S encode_zstring only the initial commit. Risk 'behaviour' is honest; the population probe in the proof decides whether any live record moves.

**Fix.** Carry the original subrecord BYTES through whenever the value is unchanged (only re-encode when a path is actually redirected), and use ONE codec (cp1252, or latin-1 for pure pass-through) for every plugin-string decode/encode in esp.py, ube_patcher.py, auto_convert.py, bsa_strings.py:443.

**Proof it landed.** Unit: feed the ARMA emitter a MOD3 of `b"meshes\\armor\\caf\xe9\\x_1.nif\x00"` with converted_nif_exists=False and assert the emitted bytes are identical to the input; same for a MAST with 0xE9. Population: a read-only probe over the live load order counting MOD2-5/MAST subrecords with any byte >= 0x80 (if 0, the change is byte-neutral on this modlist and the test is the whole proof; if >0, regenerate the patch for one such mod and diff record bytes and confirm the piece renders). Meshes are untouched - no vertex comparison applies.

**Prior art.** Not found (memory grep for encode_zstring/MAST/cp1252+plugin/non-ASCII: only the PowerShell em-dash note). BUG-12 is the recorded sibling on the XML side.
